#2843. NFL news, live updates: Kirk Cousins officially named Vikings' Week 10 starter after trade deadline swap with Raiders

The 2026 NFL trade deadline is officially in the rearview mirror, and we're on to Week 10, where we'll see a couple of players in new uniforms this week — and some in their old uniforms.

Kirk Cousins rejoined the Minnesota Vikings this week via a trade with the Las Vegas Raiders. He will officially don purple this weekend against the Green Bay Packers after head coach Kevin O'Connell officially named him the team's Week 10 starter on Friday. He'll make his return to Minnesota against the very team he last faced as a Viking, when he suffered a season-ending Achilles tear. He'll look to give the offense a much-needed boost to complement Brian Flores' defense after the team's 6-2 start.

With the trade of Cousins, the Raiders officially named 2026 No. 1 pick Fernando Mendoza as their starter moving forward. He'll face a tough schedule to kick off his career as a starting quarterback, with the Seattle Seahawks and Denver Broncos on deck in Weeks 10 and 11.

Follow along with Yahoo Sports for the latest NFL news, injury updates and more ahead of Week 10.

After a great deal of speculation was made that Jonathan Taylor could be moved ahead of the NFL trade deadline, the Indianapolis Colts are reportedly standing pat.

The team will not trade him ahead of the deadline, NFL insider Tom Pelissero reported Tuesday afternoon.

Pelissero reported that the Colts had received multiple offers for Taylor of a second-round pick-plus, but instead, the team will move forward with him as a foundational piece within the locker room as they look to rebuild.

The San Francisco 49ers traded for veteran wideout Keenan Allen this week, but they're getting another reinforcement Sunday on the road against the Dallas Cowboys. Fellow receiver Deebo Samuel Sr., who has missed the past three games while recovering from a hamstring issue, has been cleared to play.

The Niners will, however, still be without Mike Evans. His Achilles injury will keep him out for a third straight game.

As expected, the Minnesota Vikings have named Kirk Cousins their starting quarterback for Week 10's matchup against the Green Bay Packers. That will mark his first start with the organization since 2023, when his season ended prematurely after tearing his Achilles (coincidentally, against the Packers at Lambeau).

It remains unclear who will back up Cousins in Week 10. Kyler Murray has been dealing with a hamstring injury, and Carson Wentz … well, he solidified the team's need to trade for Cousins in the first place with a disastrous start against the Buffalo Bills on "Monday Night Football."

New Orleans Saints running back Travis Etienne Jr. was a full participant in Friday's practice, though he's still listed as questionable on the team's injury report with a hamstring issue.

Asked if he'll play on Sunday versus the Carolina Panthers, Etienne said, "I hope so. I definitely want to."

Etienne hasn't played since Week 3, when he rushed for 57 yards on 13 carries in a 35-27 loss to the Las Vegas Raiders. His 128 yards currently rank fourth on a Saints team that ranks second-to-last in the NFC (and fifth in the NFL) in rushing offense.

#2840. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on the growth potential of these 3 stocks

Fears around the artificial intelligence trade and higher pressure on oil prices are weighing on global markets, but a few stocks offer investors solid growth prospects even in choppy times.

Investors with a long-term horizon can look beyond the ongoing volatility and track the recommendations of top Wall Street analysts to pick attractive stocks with strong long-term growth potential.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Health insurer UnitedHealth delivered better-than-expected results for the third quarter of 2026. The company also raised its full-year earnings outlook, reflecting continued progress on its turnaround plan.

Following the Q3 print, Morgan Stanley analyst Erin Wright reiterated a buy rating on UNH stock with a price target of $529. "UNH's beat & raise reinforces turnaround momentum, with improving MA [Medicare Advantage]/Optum Health supporting a higher '26 EPS baseline & upside to '27 consensus," said Wright.

The five-star analyst added that better-than-expected performance in the UnitedHealthcare and Optum Health businesses supported another upgrade to the company's 2026 adjusted earnings per share outlook to at least $20.50. Wright noted that UNH raised the earnings guidance despite incremental investments planned for the fourth quarter of 2026.

Furthermore, Wright highlighted management's view that the consensus 2027 EPS of $22.63 was too conservative. Reaching even the low end of the company's 13% to 16% long-term EPS growth target would imply an EPS of $23.17.

Wright raised her 2026 EPS estimate to $20.65 from $19.82 and 2027 EPS to $23.24 from $22.54, reflecting the latest upside and an improving 2026 medical loss ratio to 87.4% from 88.1%. Overall, she believes that UNH's operational performance continues to improve, increasing her confidence in its UHC strategy and Optum Health turnaround execution.

Wright ranks No. 264 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been successful 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 15%. See UnitedHealth Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Social media giant Meta Platforms has been making headlines since the launch of its Muse AI agent, which is seeing solid traction. Investors look forward to updates on the company's AI strategy when it announces its third-quarter results on Oct. 21.

Heading into Q3 earnings, Guggenheim analyst Michael Morris maintained a buy rating on Meta stock and increased his price target to $850 from $700, reflecting higher estimates for 2027. He expects Muse usage momentum and engagement levels to shape investor sentiment into the Q3 earnings call.

Specifically, Morris highlighted that, according to Apptopia, Muse reached 1.3 million daily active users in five weeks versus 300,000 in the first week. Given that Muse's current adoption represents less than 0.05% of Meta's 3.6 billion daily active user base, the analyst expects management to prioritize boosting usage over near-term monetization.

"Over time, we believe Muse monetizes primarily through businesses paying to reach consumers through their agents, consistent with the economics of Meta's core advertising business, rather than consumer wallets," said the five-star analyst. He added that the checkout integrations with Shopify and PayPal reflect this framework.

While there are concerns that a rival could overtake Muse or the recently launched Muse Wonder and push Meta into a prolonged cycle of overinvestment, Morris remains bullish due to the company's strong record in scaling WhatsApp and competing strongly with TikTok via Reels as well as free distribution to an extensive user base.

Morris ranks No. 629 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Meta Platforms KPIs on TipRanks.

Next is semiconductor company Marvell Technology. Earlier this month, Marvell held its Investor Day, where it raised its total addressable market estimate to $400 billion by 2030, reflecting solid AI-led demand tailwinds.

After hosting a non-deal roadshow with Marvell's senior management following the Investor Day, Stifel analyst Tore Svanberg reiterated a buy rating on the stock with a price target of $370.

The five-star analyst said that a major focus of the roadshow was the XPU and XPU Attach mix, as Marvell's Custom business expands toward its FY31 revenue target of $30 billion. In this regard, management reiterated that it expects a balanced mix of XPU and XPU Attach revenue in FY31, though it noted that either segment could grow rapidly enough to shift the mix toward 60/40 or 40/60.

While XPU drove most of the nearly $1.5 billion Custom revenue in FY26, Svanberg expects Marvell's XPU Attach business to grow substantially through the end of the decade, leading to a more balanced mix in this business. At the Investor Day event, the company raised its FY29 Custom target from more than $10 billion to over $12 billion, mainly driven by strength in the XPU Attach business.

Svanberg said that another key topic in the roadshow was whether Marvell can secure enough capacity to meet its raised targets. Management said that it met with suppliers ahead of Investor Day to share its five-year goals and received commitments to support these targets. The analyst believes that Marvell's supply chain commitments give it a competitive edge over rivals as customers seek reliable partners for large-scale infrastructure buildouts.

Svanberg ranks No. 91 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 58% of the time, delivering an average return of 31.8%. See Marvell Technology Statistics on TipRanks.

#2839. Pelicans evaluating possibilities after trade request from Dejounte Murray, who recently rebutted criticism on social media

The New Orleans Pelicans are evaluating potential trade opportunities for Dejounte Murray after a trade request from the All-Star guard, the team announced Friday.

The news comes the same week the 30-year-old Murray rebutted criticism on social media with since-deleted posts, one of which reportedly featured profanity. The Pelicans are 4-8 this season, including 1-8 in the games Murray has appeared in.

Murray won't play in Friday night's NBA Cup group-play game between New Orleans and the Memphis Grizzlies, the Pelicans announced, citing his left ankle sprain. His agent Rich Paul and Pelicans officials were reported by ESPN's Shams Charania to be working on a resolution earlier in the day.

The Phoenix Suns have expressed interest in the 2022 All-Star, according to NBA insider Chris Haynes.

The 2026-27 campaign is his third with the Pelicans, with whom he's currently averaging 13.4 points, 4.6 rebounds, 4.4 assists and 1.6 steals per game.

That said, Murray is shooting just 29.6% from 3, and he's committed four turnovers twice so far.

In the fallout of a 145-111 home loss to the 3-8 Brooklyn Nets, the X account for Pro Pels Talk, a Pelicans podcast, posted an array of game clips, breaking down the game film without mincing words. The commentary that accompanied some of those clips questioned Murray's on-court effort.

As reported by The Athletic, Murray responded to one of those posts, which showcased footage of him apparently misplacing a pass intended for forward Saddiq Bey. The ball ended up low and away, resulting in a turnover, whereas Bey seemed to be asking for it near chest level.

"Ohhhhh let's talk about it," Murray wrote back on X in a since-deleted post, per The Athletic. "When you run post plays, are they suppose to catch on the block or the 3pt line? Let's hear it Mr social media coach, Gm and President? You have no f***ing clue what we dealing with.

"Maybe you should ask the whole team individually how they feel on a serious note and then … rethink alot of s***."

Before that, Murray engaged with an NBACentral X post that noted how the Pelicans are 3-0 without him this season.

In a quote post, Murray used a slew of crying laughing emojis and then said this, as captured in a screenshot by a social media user:

"From what's going on INSIDE This sounds like someone hit you to say this!! THE 'SABOTAGE' IS REAL!!!!!!"

On Friday, he apparently reposted Charania's report about him wanting out of New Orleans. That repost no longer appears on his X feed, however.

Charania went on "NBA Today" Friday after breaking the news to flesh it out more:

"In the last several days, I'm told, Murray has met with the organization, has made it clear to them that he wants to go somewhere else and play a meaningful, key role on another team," Charania said on the program.

"And we've seen Dejounte Murray, especially on social media, express his frustration with how things are going there. Clearly, it is not working out there. He is not going to be playing basketball in New Orleans for the time moving forward here."

Charania noted that in Murray's absence, the Pelicans will turn over the lead guard position to 2025 No. 7 overall pick Jeremiah Fears. The 20-year-old Oklahoma product has averaged 18 points and nine assists in the three games Murray has missed this season.

Murray's been available for only 54 Pelicans games since he came aboard ahead of the 2024-25 campaign. He suffered a torn Achilles that season, and that injury kept him out of a good portion of last season, too.

Murray's best NBA season occurred in 2021-22, when he earned his All-Star recognition and led the league in steals during his final go-around with the San Antonio Spurs. He also spent two seasons with the Atlanta Hawks. They ultimately traded him to the Pelicans for two first-round picks, Dyson Daniels, Larry Nance Jr., E.J. Liddell and Cody Zeller.

#2836. James Harden is foundational to the Cavs’ success

James Harden arrived to the Cleveland Cavaliers as a divisive figure.

As the centerpiece of a trade that sent fan favorite Darius Garland to the Clippers, Harden was always going to face an uphill battle.

Now, coming off an Eastern Conference Finals appearance, all eyes are on Harden as he begins his second season with the team. After twelve games, one thing is clear: his performance will determine whether the Cavaliers can improve on last year.

The vibes around Harden hit a low point after Cleveland was walloped by the Charlotte Hornets, 124-112, on November 9.

He was at the center of the team's problems. There were long stretches of minimal effort on defense, and he often looked like he was coasting, barely attempting to bring down rebounds.

Offensively, it was just as bleak: 11 points on 3-9 shooting with four turnovers. Safe to say, the fanbase was ready to drive Harden to the airport and send him to whatever team, or country, would take him.

Then came the Knicks game on November 11.

Suddenly, Harden was energized and putting his mark on the game.

He orchestrated a 115-107 road win at Madison Square Garden and put the offense on his back with 32 points, going 9-for-14 from the field with five threes and 9-11 at the free throw line.

Putting those two games back to back is as stark as it is telling of where the Cavaliers are. The team knew the risks and still entered the James Harden business.

This isn't MVP Harden, and this isn't the Houston Rockets of the 2010s, but the sentiment still stands: Cleveland is reliant on Harden to make the pieces on this roster gel. When he is at his worst, the ripples spread throughout the entire lineup.

The numbers show just how much his play matters, if not in the way Cleveland hoped. Through his first 11 games, the Cavaliers had a -5.6 net rating with Harden on the floor and a +15.7 net rating with him off it. Whatever his Knicks performance does to those numbers, it was a long way from enough to erase them.

Is this too much to ask of a 37-year-old? Yes. But the Cavaliers knew this was the route they were taking when they traded for Harden and then extended him on a three-year, $97 million deal. At times this season, it has looked like a grave mistake.

Harden is posting some of the lowest numbers of his career: 16.7 points, 5.6 assists and 4.6 rebounds per game. That scoring is about seven points below his 23.6 average last season, and about four below the 20.5 he averaged in 26 games after joining Cleveland. His assists are down more than two per game from last season's 8.0.

Still, the Knicks game suggests the player fans expect isn't gone. The problem for the Cavaliers is how to keep Harden engaged and empowered enough that performances like that don't become few and far between.

It will be a balancing act. Cleveland has to meet Harden at a level of comfort that lets him use his best traits, while melding his heliocentric style with an offense that fits the rest of the roster and lets the ball move more freely. That will take time. The Cavaliers knew this came with Harden, and fans need to understand it won't be pretty until a resolution is found on the court.

#2835. Fort Collins football stays unbeaten with big 5A playoff win

It wasn't an emphatic statement, but it got the job done.

Fort Collins High School football's special season isn't done though. Not just yet after a 39-34 win over No. 13 Northfield in the Colorado Class 5A playoffs Nov. 13 at French Field.

This was clearly a step up in competition for the fourth-seeded Lambkins (11-0) against a talented Denver team.

But the Lambkins have Jace Garcia under center, and that goes a long way.

The breakout star quarterback had another big game, going over 300 yards while accounting for four total touchdowns.

Fort Collins needed more from the junior in this one, and he delivered.

Garcia brushed off pressure from an athletic Nighthawks front, taking hits in the pocket and scrambling to extend plays.

It paid off, as he came up with some game-changing throws, as Garcia hit Donovan Dumas for a pair of crucial second-half touchdowns.

On one, he baited edge defenders, drawing them away from Dumas for a short scoring throw. On the other, the first-year starter stayed in a condensed pocket and lofted a perfect third-down pass to Dumas down the middle.

Garcia went over 300 passing yards for the eighth time this season, further proof that he's one of Colorado's best QBs this fall.

It was enough to hold off a Nighthawks squad that tested Fort Collins in new ways.

Northfield took an early lead after a fourth-down stop, made the Lambkins punt (a rarity) and held them to a field goal inside the 10-yard line (another uncommon sight).

The Fort Collins defense rose up to match the fight, though. The Lambkins forced a pair of fumbles and held Northfield's wing T-based rushing attack in relative check, including forcing two field goals.

Then, the Lambkins came up with a huge stop after Northfield got the ball back with 1:35 left in plus territory, setting off a huge celebration on the home sideline.

Fort Collins is into the 5A quarterfinals for the first time in 20 years, and it will be a big one.

No. 5 Grandview will come up north for a huge showdown and another major challenge for this unbeaten Lambkins sqaud.

#2834. OpenAI launches ChatGPT Wallet, allowing its 'dots' AI agents to make purchases for users

OpenAI on Friday announced ChatGPT Wallet, a new payment feature that enables its "dots" artificial intelligence agents to make purchases on behalf of users.

The company built ChatGPT Wallet in partnership with American Express, Mastercard and Visa, and it allows cardholders to maintain their existing credit card rewards and protections. Eligible users enter their card information once, prompt their dot to find items to buy and approve each purchase with a security check.

OpenAI said its dots agents will never actually access a user's card details. Instead, their payment networks will provide the dot with a "payment token," which contains a scoped card number, security code and expiration date that is specific to a given transaction.

"Our goal here is to really focus on merchants and our network partners and start building out a really great foundation that's safe and easy to use," Chaya Nayak, who leads product for monetization and payments at OpenAI, told CNBC in an interview.

ChatGPT Wallet comes as a growing number of consumers are exploring how to use personal AI agents to manage their calendars, book travel and complete other day-to-day tasks. OpenAI introduced the dots agents during its annual developer conference in late September, shortly after Meta's Muse offering topped the App Store charts.

Purchases made through ChatGPT Wallet could eventually kick-start a new revenue stream for OpenAI, but the company said it is not taking a cut of transactions to start. With the initial launch, OpenAI is working to build relationships with merchants and position itself as a serious platform for shoppers, especially as vendors are beginning to experiment with personal agents.

OpenAI has pushed deeper into AI-enabled commerce over the last year, but some of its early attempts failed to take off. The company introduced an "Instant Checkout" feature last fall, which it quickly pivoted away from. By March, it had rolled out a revamped shopping experience that aimed to make it easier for users to find and compare products.

Nayak said ChatGPT Wallet builds on OpenAI's previous efforts, particularly around product discovery.

"We think that this is the next step, where if someone found something they're really excited to buy, then we want to be able to get them through to that endpoint," she said.

OpenAI's ChatGPT chatbot supports more than 1.2 billion weekly active users and nearly 100 million paying users as of the end of the third quarter. With such a broad user base, Gebb said OpenAI can decide to "turn up the volume" on ChatGPT Wallet when it wants to.

"It just makes sense to watch it, start it in a contained environment, and then keep rolling from there," Gebb told CNBC in an interview.

Merchants and vendors are still reckoning with the rise of personal AI agents, and some companies have opted to forgo them entirely. United Airlines said Wednesday that it will block agents from accessing its systems to book tickets, and Amazon prevents the agents from purchasing products on its platform.

OpenAI said Friday that agentic commerce is still in its "early days," and it published tools and guidance alongside its ChatGPT Wallet announcement to help businesses start to navigate the transition. The company explained how merchants can make sure their websites aren't inadvertently blocking OpenAI's agents, for instance.

"It's going to take time for the ecosystem to build the right infrastructure to be able to make this experience really amazing and seamless across all of their sites," Nayak said. "Really, who we are speaking to here is the networks and the merchants."

#2833. Vikings name Week 10 starter vs Packers after Kirk Cousins trade

The Minnesota Vikings finally made the announcement that many have been expecting for several days.

Quarterback Kirk Cousins will start against the Green Bay Packers in Week 10, head coach Kevin O'Connell announced on Friday, Nov. 13. The intra-divisional game will mark the start of Cousins' second stint in Minnesota, three years after his last game for the Vikings.

"Kirk Cousins will start the football game. Cannot give him enough credit for coming in here, how he's gone about the week. Both him and the coaching staff getting him ready to go, working extremely hard, using all the time we possibly can," O'Connell said.

"It's been really impressive, what he's been able to do ... just getting right back in the huddle with a lot of guys he's played with, but certainly some guys he hasn't. It's been something that everybody's been very, very impressed by. Excited to start Kirk in the game, see him play on Sunday."

KIRK COUSINS: Veteran QB kept mum about his chance to start Week 10

Coincidentally, the upcoming Nov. 15 clash will be Cousins' second straight matchup against the Packers as the Vikings' starting quarterback. The last game the 38-year-old played for Minnesota, in 2023, was also at Lambeau Field.

Cousins' first stint with the Vikings ended unceremoniously – trainers had to cart him off of the field after he tore his Achilles during a game his team went on to win. In the offseason that followed, the Vikings allowed him to walk in free agency, and he went on to sign a four-year, $180 million contract with the Atlanta Falcons.

Cousins said in his (re-)introductory press conference with the Vikings on Nov. 11 that he expects the Week 10 game to be meaningful. It's both his first game back with Minnesota but also his return to Lambeau Field for the first time since the 2023 injury.

"It means a lot to play at Lambeau Field, no matter what the circumstances," Cousins said. "Knowing that that was where I was carted off, and I had thoughts of, 'Is this my last game? Is this my last game as a Viking?' To be back out there, wearing the purple and gold. It has meaning, but you have to go make it mean something with the way you play."

In nine starts with the Las Vegas Raiders this season, Cousins has a 4-5 record. He was 202-of-315 passing for 2,340 yards, 20 touchdowns and eight interceptions. His 97.1 passer rating so far this season is his highest since his previous stint with Minnesota.

The Vikings are hoping the addition of Cousins will help kick-start an offensive resurgence. The team has lost two straight following a 6-0 start, and it sits in third place in a tightly contested NFC North.

While the defense has been a strong point – the 14.5 points per game the unit has allowed so far is the fewest in the league – the offense has struggled. Minnesota's 18.3 points scored per game are sixth-fewest in the NFL.

#2832. Grizzlies tie NBA record, move back into NBA Cup contention with Pelicans win

A 31-point loss to the Oklahoma City Thunder on Nov. 6 felt like a dagger to the Memphis Grizzlies' NBA Cup hopes.

Even though it was Memphis' first group-play game, a deficit that large in a quick tournament where point differential matters appeared to put the Grizzlies in a difficult position.

The Grizzlies responded the only way they could.

Memphis went on the road against the New Orleans Pelicans on Nov. 13 and won 142-111 at Smoothie King Center.

That's right. The 31-point victory has the Grizzlies (8-4) back in NBA Cup contention.

The win was about more than making up ground in the tournament, though. Memphis also improved to 6-0 on the road this season, extending a franchise record to start the season.

The Grizzlies' first loss of the season came against New Orleans, a 135-134 overtime defeat on Nov. 2. Memphis responded emphatically, dominating the Pelicans in all four quarters this time around.

The Grizzlies have a quick turnaround, hosting the Brooklyn Nets on Nov. 14 at FedExForum (7 p.m. CT, DAZN-MEM).

Here's what stood out from Memphis' 31-point win over New Orleans.

Grizzlies center Zach Edey is still being brought along carefully after an ankle procedure cut his campaign short last season. He has not yet been cleared to play both ends of a back-to-back, so Memphis rested him against New Orleans.

Isaiah Stewart stepped in and held down the center position.

Stewart finished with 12 points, five rebounds and four blocks in 20 minutes, providing a strong defensive presence while Edey rested.

Like Stewart, veteran forward Jerami Grant was acquired by the Grizzlies in an offseason trade. He provided a significant offensive boost against the Pelicans, finishing with a team-high 22 points on 7-for-10 shooting and three 3-pointers.

Grant and Stewart are examples of the Grizzlies' depth, but Memphis' production against New Orleans went well beyond those two.

The Grizzlies outscored the Pelicans, 76-27, in bench points.

Cam Spencer and GG Jackson each added 14 points off the bench, while Taylor Hendricks recorded a double-double with 10 points and 12 rebounds.

Memphis also set a franchise record and tied an NBA record with 10 players scoring in double figures.

And just like that, the Grizzlies are back in the hunt.

The 31-point victory was pivotal because of the importance of point differential in determining the NBA Cup standings. It gives Memphis a chance to win its group or potentially qualify for the knockout round as a wild card.

The Grizzlies have two group-play games remaining: at home against the Los Angeles Clippers and on the road against the Minnesota Timberwolves.

Memphis will likely need to win both to have a strong chance of advancing.

The game against Minnesota could be particularly important. The Timberwolves are currently 2-0 in group play with a plus-32 point differential.

After taking a 31-point hit against Oklahoma City, Memphis has now essentially erased the deficit with one emphatic response.

Damichael Cole is the Memphis Grizzlies beat writer for The Commercial Appeal. Contact Damichael at damichael.cole@commercialappeal.com. Follow Damichael on X, formerly known as Twitter, @DamichaelC.ge

This article originally appeared on Memphis Commercial Appeal: Grizzlies tie NBA record, move back into NBA Cup contention with Pelicans win

#2831. The Goal of the Utah Jazz This Season: Win The NBA Cup

If you follow me on TikTok or Twitter you may already know this about me, but for those who don't: I am a BIG NBA Cup supporter. We #RespectTheCup in this house. I am also a big soccer (or as I call it, footy) fan, so I was already prone to enjoying these in-season tournaments. When it was announced I actually think that I was one of the only ones in my content creation circle, and certainly the only one in my friend group, to be 100% on board with the idea.

While Adam Silver has had more missteps in the last few years than I would prefer, he knocked it out of the park with the NBA Cup. As mentioned above, I think that there are ways to improve the tournament, but even in its short existence, it has brought much needed excitement and punch to the early portion of the NBA season.

The Utah Jazz haven't had the season that any of us would have envisioned, and while starting 1-11 is quite the hole to put ourselves in, the NBA Cup allows us to strive towards a real goal outside of "development." Even more important than presenting an opportunity to lift up some hardware, getting all the way to the NBA Cup Final would prove that this team is ready to compete one day.

The NBA Cup's lineage is relatively short, but in its brief history we've been able to see some truly great basketball teams compete for the prize. If we take a trip down memory lane, we will notice something almost immediately — the teams that make it are/were threats to the rest of the league.

These weren't flukes, they were mistakes; if you made it to the Final, you were a team with the aspirations and the roster to play basketball in June.

The inaugural NBA Cup Final was a success by every single metric there is. The game drew 4.58 million viewers between ABC and ESPN, the arena in Las Vegas hosted over 19,000 fans, and the Final featured one of the most exciting teams in the league at the time in the Indiana Pacers and the one of the most well known brands in all of sports in the Los Angeles Lakers, of course led by LeBron James – one of the most recognizable athletes in the world.

While the Lakers weren't world beaters at this time, being three years removed from a championship, the year prior this squad lost in the Western Conference Finals to the ever dangerous Denver Nuggets. On the other side of the bracket, Tyrese Haliburton and the Indiana Pacers were the talk of the town. Haliburton was coming off of an All-Star campaign the year prior and the Pacers were just so fun to watch. The matchup also presented an opportunity for there to be a "passing of the torch" moment, but LeBron had other plans.

LeBron finished the game with 24 points, 11 rebounds, and 4 assists, Anthony Davis had a game of a lifetime with 41 points and 20 rebounds, and Austin Reaves chipped in 28 points off the bench in route to a 123-109 victory. LeBron won the inaugural NBA Cup MVP award, the most recognizable NBA team in the world won the inaugural NBA Cup, and the win reiterated that the Los Angeles Lakers were still going to compete at the highest level.

While I was personally rooting for the Indiana Pacers in this game, they gave it everything they had. Haliburton had a cool 20 point and 11 assist performance while Bennedict Mathurin scored 20 off the bench. It wasn't the result that they (or I) wanted, but it wasn't long before they proved to be worthy opponents themselves. Lest we forget that the Pacers could have been one catastrophic injury away from lifting up the Larry O'Brien Trophy the following year in the 2024-2025 NBA Season.

The second iteration of the NBA Cup had quite the shoes to fill, but the NBA Cup Final did not disappoint. While there wasn't a widely recognizable brand in the Final like the Lakers or the Celtics, there was a massive amount of star power.

The Milwaukee Bucks were just a few years removed from a NBA Championship themselves, and Giannis Antetokounmpo was out to prove that they could still compete. Giannis was, and still is, one of the the world's best players and one of the game's biggest names. With Damian Lillard at his side, Giannis was out to prove that the Bucks were still threats to come out of the East.

Over in the West, the Oklahoma City Thunder were taking the league by storm. The year prior the team showed up on everyone's radar after winning 57 games, and this year turned out to be no different. After it was all said and done, the OKC Thunder won 68 games led by Shai Gilgeous-Alexander who won his first MVP award, and the crew hoisted the Larry O'Brien Trophy at the end of the year.

Unfortunately for the Thunder, Giannis and the Bucks weren't ready to lose this night. Giannis lead the way with a 26 point, 19 rebound, and 10 assist triple-double while Damian Lillard scored 23 points and led the Bucks to a 97-81 victory.

The Power of Friendship didn't help the Thunder on this night, but they did give it their best effort: SGA scored 21, Jalen Williams scored 18,and Isaiah Hartenstein chipped in 16. While they weren't victorious this night, they did prove to the world that they could compete with anyone in the league. After making it all the way to the NBA Cup Final, they dominated the regular season and defeated the aforementioned Indiana Pacers in 7 games in the NBA Finals.

The third iteration of the NBA Cup was arguably the best of the three. It also combined the best of both worlds from the previous two matchups: mega star power AND a recognizable brand. The New York Knicks are either the second or third (depending on who you ask) most recognizable brand in all of basketball, and for good reason. They don't call Madison Square Garden "The Mecca of Basketball" for no reason. To top it all off, the Knicks were coming off of a 51 win season and a Conference Finals appearance the previous year, and Jalen Brunson just made the All-NBA 2nd Team.

Out west the San Antonio Spurs were well ahead of schedule with their roster. Stephon Castle just won the Rookie of the Year award the previous year, they drafted Dylan Harper with the #2 overall pick in the 2025 NBA Draft, and Victor Wembanyama had displayed All-World potential despite missing most of the 2024-2025 NBA season with DVT. To a lesser extent compared to the Knicks, the Spurs are also a world wide brand in the basketball realm due to the success of international stars like Tony Parker and Manu Ginobili.

With all of the star power and recognizable brands at play, it shouldn't be a surprise that the Amazon Prime Video broadcast averaged over 3 million viewers. What also wasn't a surprise is that both teams came to play. The Spurs had 7 players in double figures; Wemby and Dylan Harper combined for 39 points off the bench, while Stephon Castle scored 15 points and dropped 12 dimes as a starter. For the Knicks, their Power of Friendship mantra rang true with 7 players scoring in double digits led by Jalen Brunson's 25 points and OG Anunoby's 28 points.

The Knicks came out on top 124-113, but this was just the appetizer for the NBA Finals. In the NBA Cup Final both teams proved that they had what it took to compete at the highest level, and then they both powered their way through the NBA Playoffs to meet in the NBA Finals. Unfortunately for The Baseline Bums, the Knicks prevailed again in a 4-1 series win to secure a championship for the first time in 53 years.

Time and time again, the NBA Cup has proven to be the battleground for future competitors and NBA Championship threats. In all three years of existence, the teams competing in the NBA Cup have either already climbed to the mountain top or they were getting ready to take the league by storm. THAT is what we should be striving for. I think after the 1-11 start it would be very difficult to compete for a Playoff spot (I predicted that we would finish 7th) so we should strive to do something more enticing than another year of developing our young talent.

We had higher expectations for our team, but we've had a season from hell when it comes to the injury department: Jaren Jackson Jr. is out until the new year, Jusuf Nurkic will be out for at least another two weeks, and Lauri Markkanen will miss at least a month with his hand fracture. Do I think that it will be difficult to win the games necessary to advance in the NBA Cup tournament without them? Absolutely. Do I think that this is a tremendous chance for our young players to prove what they can do? Without a doubt.

The road will be hard, especially after dropping our first NBA Cup game against the Mavericks 112-100, but I don't think that our chance to win has been snuffed out. Tonight we play the Houston Rockets, and while they are formidable, they are not unbeatable. After that, the Suns and Nuggets are both very volatile. There is a real scenario where the Jazz go 3-1 in group play and earn a spot in the NBA Cup Quarter Finals. Once we get there, it just takes a hot streak to take us all the way to the Promised Land.

Again, without Lauri or JJJ, the burden will be placed on our younger players and luckily we have the firepower to do it. Keyonte George is good for 20 points a game this year, Darryn Peterson became the first teenager in Utah Jazz history to score 20+ points in four straight games, and Ace Bailey has proven that he is invaluable to this team.

The season has been disappointing so far, but there are reasons to be excited and goals to strive for. There is a lot of basketball left to play this year, and I for one am not satisfied with chalking up this year to another development year. Our goal for the rest of the year should be to win the NBA Cup, because if we can do that with a depleted roster, just imagine what we can do in the 2027-2028 season with a fully heathy squad.

How do you feel about the NBA Cup? How do you think the Jazz will fare the rest of the season? Sound off in the comments below!

#2830. Chiefs-Falcons Madden Simulation Week 10: On the Road Again

On Sunday, the (6-2) Kansas City Chiefs are on the road again taking on the (3-6) Atlanta Falcons. Let's see how the game plays out in Madden NFL 27.

Note: Madden simulation is 7-1 this season, predicting the outcome.

If you want to watch the simulation without spoilers, start this video — or if you can't see it, click here.

The Falcons' opening drive gained just seven yards before they were forced to punt on their first possession.

The Chiefs' ground-and-pound attack erupted on their first drive, highlighted by a 28-yard run from Kenneth Walker. Walker rushed for 36 yards on the drive, while backup running back Emmett Johnson added 13 yards on a third-and-10 run to move the chains.

The drive would end with Harrison Butker drilling a 44-yard field goal to open the scoring.

End of first quarter: Chiefs 3, Falcons 0

The Falcons opened the quarter at the Chiefs' 19-yard line. Drake London caught a 15-yard pass to reach the four-yard line. From the pistol formation, Michael Penix Jr. handed the ball to Bijan Robinson, who ran to the right, broke a tackle, and found the end zone for a touchdown.

The Chiefs failed to reach the first-down marker on their next possession and were forced to punt the ball back to Atlanta.

A 14-yard pick-and-roll pass to Jahan Dotson moved the ball to the Chiefs' 14-yard line. Bijan Robinson finished the drive by catching a pass from Penix Jr. and scoring his second touchdown of the game.

The Chiefs found themselves at midfield with a minute remaining in the quarter. On third-and-three, Rashee Rice ran a comeback route and picked up the first down to keep the drive alive. Mahomes then connected with newly acquired Josh Downs for a 12-yard gain and another first down.

The Chiefs managed to put points on the board before halftime as Butker drilled his second field goal of the game, this time from 44 yards out, cutting the Falcons' lead to eight.

End of second quarter: Falcons 14, Chiefs 6

Josh Downs hauled in an 18-yard pass on a deep out route, going up to make the catch while keeping both feet in bounds. The reception moved the Chiefs to the Falcons' 25-yard line.

Two plays later, Xavier Watts intercepted Mahomes on a high throw intended for Rashee Rice running a crossing route.

The Falcons turned to their running game to drain the clock, alternating between Bijan Robinson and Brian Robinson.

Another 11-yard run from Bijan Robinson moved Atlanta to the Chiefs' 11-yard line. Brina Robinson followed it up with a 10-yard run to carry the Falcons into the fourth quarter.

End of third quarter: Falcons 14, Chiefs 6

A toss to Bijan Robinson went untouched into the end zone for his third touchdown of the game, extending Atlanta's lead.

The Chiefs found themselves at their own 48-yard line facing a third-and-five. From the shotgun, Mahomes delivered a strike on a fly route to Jalen Royals, who hauled in the pass for a touchdown to bring the game back within one score.

The Falcons reached midfield on their next possession but were stopped at the Chiefs' 48-yard line and forced to punt.

Mahomes connected with Travis Kelce for 16 yards to move the Chiefs to their own 48-yard line.

Downs followed with a 26-yard reception on an out route.

Royals then hauled in a 17-yard pass on a deep crossing route to move the Chiefs to the nine-yard line.

Mahomes went back to the player who helped get the drive started. Kelce was targeted inside the red zone and came down with the touchdown to bring Kansas City closer.

The Chiefs attempted a two-point conversion to tie the game, but the Falcons stopped a screen pass intended for Johnson.

Kansas City's defense came up with a three-and-out on the Falcons' next possession, giving the Chiefs another opportunity. Atlanta was forced to punt, leaving Kansas City with one timeout and eight seconds remaining before the two-minute warning.

The Chiefs crossed midfield after Mahomes connected with Kelce for a 14-yard gain. However, the next play went backward as Mahomes was sacked for an eight-yard loss.

The sack put Kansas City in a fourth-and-13 situation with 15 seconds remaining.

The Chiefs' final attempt fell incomplete as Mahomes tried to make a throw while being taken down by the pass rush. Kansas City turned the ball over on downs, ending its comeback attempt.

#2828. Supreme Court agrees to hear Robinhood challenge to IPO information omission lawsuit

The Supreme Court on Friday agreed to hear a case in which Robinhood seeks to block a class-action lawsuit over the omission of interim financial data from the online broker's initial public offering in 2021.

Robinhood wants the Supreme Court to overturn a ruling by the 9th Circuit U.S. Court of Appeals that revived a lawsuit by investors against the company and firms involved with the IPO, including Goldman Sachs and J.P. Morgan Securities.

A San Francisco federal court judge had previously dismissed the lawsuit.

The Trump administration and the Securities and Exchange Commission had urged the Supreme Court to hear Robinhood's appeal.

"We are pleased the Supreme Court has agreed to hear our case and look forward to presenting our argument," a Robinhood spokesperson said in a statement.

CNBC has requested comment from the attorneys representing the plaintiffs in the case.

Robinhood's IPO came on the heels of a surge in investor interest during the Covid-19 pandemic in so-called meme stocks, and trading in the crypto asset dogecoin.

In July 2021, Robinhood conducted its IPO, "for which it prepared a registration statement" that "contained only limited information about Robinhood's performance during the second quarter of 2021," the 9th Circuit panel that heard the case noted.

"After the initial public offering, Robinhood reported financial results from that second quarter, and its stock price dropped," the panel wrote.

The investors who sued Robinhood and the other defendants alleged the registration statement omitted material information it was legally required to disclose.

He didn't say anything about scoring touchdowns. Guess he didn't have to. That part was probably understood.

Beaulieu scored three touchdowns, including two game-changers early in the fourth quarter, to help propel Southington past Maloney 38-14 at Falcon Field.

The Blue Knights led by just 17-14 when Beaulieu took an inside screen from quarterback Jacoby Roman on the first snap of the final frame and turned it into a 45-yard, reverse-field excursion to the end zone.

Beaulieu's 61-yard breakaway five minutes later, coupled with a 37-yard TD run by senior Nick Andrews, made it a runaway down the stretch as Southington scored 31 unanswered points in all to bounce back from last week's loss to New Britian to improve to 8-1.

#2825. Wholesale prices rose 0.3% in October, putting annual rate at 6.5%, the highest in nearly four years

Wholesale prices increased less than expected in October, but inflation pressures persisted and the Federal Reserve is likely still on target for an interest rate increase soon.

The producer price index, which measures the prices domestic producers get for their products before they reach the consumer, rose a seasonally adjusted 0.3% for the month, against the Dow Jones consensus for a 0.4% increase, the Bureau of Labor Statistics reported Friday.

Stripping out food and energy, core PPI posted a 0.3% gain, meeting expectations.

On an annual basis, headline PPI accelerated 6.5%, up 0.2 percentage point for the highest annual rate since November 2022.

The report comes just a few weeks ahead of the Federal Reserve's next policy meeting, Dec. 8-9. Markets are fully pricing in a quarter percentage point increase in the central bank's key overnight borrowing rate, with slight odds that the Federal Open Market Committee might opt for a half-point hike.

Stock market futures added to gains following the release while Treasury yields were mixed.

Earlier this week, the BLS reported that inflation at the cash register was hotter than expected.

The headline consumer price index saw a 0.4% increase in October while core was up 0.3%, both 0.1 percentage point higher than expected. Because of the government shutdown in October 2025, the BLS couldn't collect some data and refrained from putting out a 12-month inflation figure for CPI.

However, the figures showed that the Fed remains a good distance from its 2% inflation goal.

"Today's PPI data – which shows some signs of improving, albeit still too-high – is unlikely to change the Fed's approach in December because the CPI data was already outside their comfort zone," said Chris Zaccarelli, chief investment officer for Northlight Asset Management.

Moreover, policymakers are beginning to worry that inflation generated by the Iran war and higher fuel prices, along with President Donald Trump's tariffs, are beginning to spread through the economy. The Fed generally would look through such price pressures as temporary, but the breadth of inflation could indicate that inflation is becoming more entrenched.

"We've had some signs of trouble now for several months in a row," Chicago Fed President Austan Goolsbee said in a CNBC interview Friday. "If you look at how broad-based the inflation is, it's not just that we're above the target and have been for some time. It's that in categories that are not driven by oil prices and not driven by tariffs, you're still seeing stalled progress and in some cases going the wrong way."

Stripping out food, energy and trade services, PPI saw an increase of 0.5% in October.

Goods prices increased 0.5% in October, with more than 60% of that move driven by goods excluding food and energy, which actually saw a 0.1% decrease. On an annual basis, the goods index jumped 10.2%, the biggest 12-month surge since October 2022.

Final demand services prices increased 0.2%, most of which was linked to transportation and warehousing services, and within that category a 3.9% gain in truck transportation of freight.

"We've been above 2% for almost six years, and while it sometimes we were making progress, lately we're not making any progress, and if anything, it's going the wrong way," Goolsbee said. "Inflation is rising; it's proved far more persistent than we predicted at the beginning from the supply shocks."

Other items in the PPI report also showed multiyear gains. Prices for intermediate processed goods soared 14.6% from October 2025, the biggest annual gain since July 2022. Intermediate prices for services posted a 6.9% increase, the most since November 2022.

#2824. Top Wall Street analysts prefer these 3 dividend stocks for stable income

Global stock markets remain volatile amid persistent geopolitical tensions and concerns about the sustainability of AI trade. Given this backdrop, investors seeking steady income can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios.

Tracking recommendations from top Wall Street analysts can help investors pick attractive stocks, as these experts assess a company's ability to pay dividends consistently.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Integrated oil and gas giant Chevron (CVX) is a dividend king. The company has increased its dividend for 39 consecutive years. At a quarterly dividend of $1.78 per share (annualized dividend of $7.12 per share), CVX offers a yield of 3.14%. Chevron will announce its third-quarter results on October 30.

In an earnings preview note on U.S. oil majors, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Chevron with a price target of $241. The analyst is focused on the company's international growth prospects across Latin America, the Middle East, West Africa, and the Eastern Mediterranean.

Specifically, Mehta highlighted management's commentary on solid execution in Venezuela, with gross production expected to more than double to nearly 600 kbd (kilo barrels per day) by 2031 via three joint ventures. He also noted a recent oil and gas condensate discovery in offshore Angola and is awaiting commercial updates on the West Qurna 2 and Nasiriyah projects in Iraq.

Furthermore, Mehta expects CVX to report improved productivity and capital efficiency across domestic shale assets. He expects the improvement mainly in the Permian Basin, where Chevron projects advanced chemical treatments to drive about a 10% recovery uplift in new wells and bring down capital spend per barrel by about 25% year-over-year. Overall, Mehta expects Chevron to report Q3 2026 EPS of $4.95, above the consensus of $4.89.

"While unfavorable timing effects and volatile commodity prices may present quarterly earnings headwinds, we highlight healthy U.S. refining margins and strong asset utilization as partial offsets," said Mehta.

Mehta ranks No. 381 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 12%. See Chevron KPIs on TipRanks.

Kinetik will announce its Q3 earnings on November 4, with a conference call scheduled the next day.

"As natural gas takeaway constraints ease across the Permian, investor focus centers on KNTK's YE26 volume trajectory and durability of its multi-year growth," said Mackay.

#2823. They train people to avoid 'honeypot' scams—here are the common red flags they spot: 'It feels so good to be wanted'

Shawnee Delaney often kicks off a training session by telling a room full of corporate workers: "OK, raise your hand if you've ever gotten a flattering message on LinkedIn just complimenting your background and your profile and your experience."

"Every hand goes up," she says. Then, she tells attendees how responding to a simple adulatory message could unwittingly open the door to a potential romance scam.

Delaney is a corporate security expert, well-versed in teaching people how to avoid “honeypot” schemes, in which a bad actor uses romantic or sexual interest to build trust and gain information, access or leverage over a target. The term is currently making headlines in the wake of former Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang's resignation from the company, following reporting on his admitted affair with an alleged Chinese spy.

Honeypots, also known as "honey traps," are a very human form of social engineering attack, a category that also includes various forms of phishing and use of deepfake technology. And you don't have to be a billionaire tech CEO to possess information that scammers may value.

As competition in the global artificial intelligence race heats up, OpenAI is reportedly training employees to recognize espionage tactics including bribery, romantic manipulation and blackmail, according to The Wall Street Journal. On Nov. 3, cryptocurrency exchange Binance revealed that a recent cyberattack — which saw roughly $6 billion of digital assets stolen — was partially made possible by a fake job interview scheme that infected an employee’s work computer with malware.

"People can get solicited, bribed, romanced, etc.," says Kevin Tian, co-founder and CEO of Doppel, an AI-powered platform meant to help identify and deflect social engineering attacks. He adds: "If you've got access to sensitive IP [intellectual property] — it's not just AI companies — you can be a target."

Increasingly, scammers can use AI to scour websites ranging from Reddit to Pinterest for an individual's personal information and customize a ploy designed specifically for them, Tian says. Once they have relevant data points, attackers tend to use "the oldest tricks in the book: elicitation, flattery, ego," says Delaney, a former intelligence officer with the Defense Intelligence Agency and the founder and CEO of Washington, D.C.-based corporate security consulting firm Vaillance Group.

In many cases, Delaney notes, targets have an inkling that something is suspicious — but they'd rather allow themselves to believe the interest is legitimate. So how do you keep yourself from falling victim? Trust your self-awareness, common sense and ability to identify situations that feel too good to be true, she says.

"The bottom line is, we're all human," says Delaney, adding: "It feels so good to be wanted, or to be respected, or to be appreciated, or loved."

Delaney, who has worked with Fortune 500 companies such as Uber and Merck, advises corporate clients on cybersecurity risks and develops training programs to improve employees' awareness of security threats. Her sessions aim to build awareness of manipulative connections, such as romance scams, from how they begin to the natural progression they often take. "Introduction, then repeated contact, favors, sensitive requests — the secrecy, the pressure," she says.

Bad actors may tap into any information you post online to find an interest that can be mirrored and exploited, or an acquaintance who can be infiltrated and make an introduction. If someone you know and trust makes an introduction, you might be less likely to "go do due diligence" on the new person, even if they're a stranger, Delaney says.

One of Vaillance's services includes building a "targeting package" around an executive or C-Suite member, says Delaney. "We can show how they are vulnerable and how we would target that organization just by leveraging the people around them in that circle: spouses, friends, kids, parents, administrative assistants, neighbors," she says. "That's when people's eyes open."

From there, she discusses red flags to spot. Conversations that begin with flattery and seemingly innocuous small talk can gradually shift toward digging for information, introductions to colleagues or access to documents, says Delaney. It's human nature to want to be helpful and fulfill friendly requests, she adds. Add a potential romantic connection to the mix, especially amid an ongoing "loneliness epidemic," and the urge to comply can be even harder to resist, she says.

If you feel yourself getting cornered in a situation where a recent acquaintance or romantic partner keeps asking for information you're not comfortable revealing, Delaney says it helps to have first practiced polite refusal. "One of my favorites is: They could just respond with: 'Why do you ask?'" she says. "Put the other person on the defensive. You could give a nondescript answer. You can say you don't know. You can say you can't respond."

If you're ever suspicious of a new connection — even if you don't want to be — do yourself a favor and conduct some basic vetting, she recommends. You might examine their social media profiles and other publicly available information, for example, to see if their story checks out.

If you can't find enough information to allay your suspicions, don't be shy about alerting your employer's security or human resources team, she says. For smaller companies, or scams outside the workplace, you can file a complaint for cyber threats or romance scams with the Federal Bureau of Investigation's Internet Crime Complaint Center.

And pay attention if your new paramour starts asking you specific questions about your finances or work credentials, says Delaney.

"If you have no game at home, and you get off an airplane somewhere and suddenly everyone's all over you, we call that a red flag," she says.

He just wants opportunities to compete, and he's out there competing. And if it helps the Buffalo Bills, that's what he's about. So, that's why we love him and that's why — the trade was not an indication of Damar Hamlin, and more so just an opportunity for us to improve our football team."

Though Hooker will, in fact, make his Bills debut in Week 10, it's possible we still see Hamlin in action on defense depending on the situation. He may also reclaim a significant role on special teams, where the last two games Hamlin played zero snaps with the special teams units.

#2821. Dejounte Murray’s trade request creates an opportunity for the Suns

Over the first eleven games of the season, it has not all been sunshine and rainbows for this Suns team. They have seen injuries, rebounding issues, and lapses on both ends of the floor. This has brought them to a 5-6 record, which lets them beat the bad teams but struggle against the good ones. As Espo from PHNX said the other night, they are basically in basketball purgatory right now, and some things need to change. Regardless of whether it's a lineup switch, plugging in Jordan Goodwin or Collin Gillespie for Jalen Green, or potentially a trade on the horizon.

In last night's contest against the Charlotte Hornets, we saw Jalen Green benched and not finish the game. This move allowed the Suns to regain their lead and still win a game they should have controlled the whole way. Since Green hasn't been playing well in the lineup and has been forced into this playmaking role, many are making him the scapegoat for the team's early-season struggles.

While they are right to put most of the blame on him, it is not entirely his fault, as sometimes the bigs have trouble rebounding or some of the forwards are not locked in defensively.

This is where Green has become a devalued asset across the league, as they now know he cannot fit with this starting lineup, and the Suns will likely switch things up as we get closer to the deadline. With Green still 24, some lottery teams will likely be willing to take a chance on him and let him shine with their club. For the Suns, today's news may have made that landing spot clearer.

Dejounte Murray of the New Orleans Pelicans has requested a trade. I've highlighted him multiple times as a good target for the Suns for many reasons, but now that his value is depleted and he's on the market, this is the perfect trade for both teams. Both teams have an asset with depreciated value that could help the other franchise, and everyone seems to be catching on.

They've also been linked to Murray via Chris Haynes in this tweet. Haynes was the first to address locker-room concerns via the Suns, saying many felt the team needed a true point guard. Well, Murray could come in and fill that role as a better fit for the starters, but also mesh into the defensive identity that Jordan Ott wants to bring to this Phoenix team,

Murray, who has a $30.8 million player option next season, would accept it heading into next offseason but at 30, doesn't seem to want a long-term, expensive deal. On the other hand, Green is young and looking to extend long term. With Booker's contract coming up, plus the Brooks extension and a potential Bridges extension, and Beal's dead money, it doesn't seem feasible to pay all four players, especially when they look this bad on the court together.

Green is also the same player option as Murray, just at $36 million, and is making that same amount this season, so not only would you save $4 million this season and $6 million next year, but you would save yourself from a pain-inducing contract negotiation. It is clear Green can't be a starter given his recent play, and Murray, who has been a head case in NOLA, may be the same, but it's an unknown.

I feel Murray, who has been frustrated with the Pelicans this season, will buy into the Suns' culture once he gets here. As we know, the Pelicans are bad and lack a clear direction for building. They view Herb Jones and Trey Murphy III as high pieces but not the building blocks of their rebuild. It seems like there are three timelines: Jones and Murphy III, Zion Williamson, and Murray as the vets who can help with a playoff push, and then you've got Jeremiah Fears and Derik Queen as the young building blocks. Getting a young player like Green may not be the best fit, but it would give him a chance to return to his scoring form and try to become their number-one offensive option.

For the Suns, this move frees up some money and gives you a player who can hopefully buy into the culture and help alleviate some of the pain for your best player. Murray isn't coming in as a game-changing secondary option, but if he can buy into the team role and do the little things, he could find some great success in this starting lineup. Murray was an all-caliber defender in San Antonio, and yeah, he's had many injuries, but I still think some of that player is still in him if he gets to Phoenix.

Ultimately, time will tell whether the Suns make this move or switch their starting lineups first. One good thing to know, though, is that Murray wants out, and the Suns look like one of the only teams that would need a true point guard. With both Green and Murray at their lowest values and on similar contracts, it seems a one-for-one deal could be the easiest thing for both parties. That said, the Pelicans front office doesn't have the best track record with trades, so maybe the Suns can get something else thrown in for taking on their disgruntled player, since Green hasn't made his frustrations as obvious as Murray's. Could the Suns try to get a pick back in exchange for a younger asset? That's something I'd like to see them try to pull off, alongside bringing in Murray to give them more flexibility in the long term.

What are your thoughts? Is Dejounte Murray someone you would like the Suns to go after? Is there another piece you think would be a better fit? Ultimately, given what assets the Suns have to trade, this may be the best option they can pursue without hurting their depth too much, and I'd like it to materialize.

#2819. Retirees sometimes tap savings for one-off expenses. Here’s what advisors say to consider first

Retirees facing a large expense that's not in their regular household budget may turn to their traditional 401(k) or individual retirement account balances to cover it.

While retirement savings may be the easiest place to turn for the money, financial advisors say there are a few things worth considering before making one-off withdrawals.

"Be aware of the fact that it's not as simple as just taking out the amount of money that [covers] what you want it for," said certified financial planner Victoria Trumbower, managing member of Trumbower Financial Advisors in Bethesda, Maryland. The firm ranked No. 19 on CNBC's Financial Advisor 100 list for 2026.

Read coverage of CNBC's 2026 Financial Advisor 100 list, including stories featuring ranked firms and their advisors:

At the same time, research shows that many retirees aren't even spending their nest egg. About a third still have 100% or more of their initial assets when they reach their mid-80s, according to a May study by the Employee Benefit Research Institute, a nonpartisan research group.

"Retirement savings are meant to be spent," said CFP Alex Canellopoulos, director of wealth and a partner with Vista Capital Partners in Portland, Oregon. "A one-time withdrawal isn't necessarily a bad decision, as long as it fits within a sustainable retirement plan."

In 2025, about 15% of 401(k) owners ages 60 to 72 with balances of at least $100,000 who had left their employer made an ad hoc withdrawal from their account, according to Vanguard's How America Retires 2026 report, which analyzed more than 125,000 ex-plan participants.

This age group falls between the onset of penalty-free withdrawals — age 59½ — and the threshold for starting required minimum distributions — currently age 73. RMDs are amounts that retirees must withdraw annually from traditional 401(k)s and IRAs.

Not all 401(k) plans allow occasional withdrawals from ex-employees — about 43% did in 2025, up from 16% in 2015, the Vanguard study says. If a plan doesn't offer such ad hoc distributions, a former employee who wants to tap that money would have to withdraw or roll over the entire balance. Typically, they roll it over to an IRA and take withdrawals from there.

The most common reasons for retirees taking irregular distributions, based on a 2025 Vanguard survey of 8,000 clients noted in the recent report, are paying off debt, with 31% of respondents citing it; covering unforeseen expenses such as medical care and home repairs, noted by 23%; and discretionary spending, with 22% citing it as a reason.

Regardless of why you take the money, advisors say a key consideration is the impact on your tax situation.

Distributions from a traditional 401(k) or IRA are generally taxed as ordinary income. If you don't have other means to cover the tax bill, you may have to withdraw extra from your retirement account to compensate — which creates a tricky situation.

"It's like a double whammy because you have to pay income tax, but you don't have any money to pay income tax, so you've got to withdraw more money to pay the tax, and then you've got to pay taxes" on the extra amount, Trumbower said.

For illustration: If you need $50,000 after taxes and assume a combined federal and state income tax rate of 25%, the math says you would need to withdraw about $66,667. That's because the money withdrawn to cover the tax bill is itself taxable. A $66,667 withdrawal taxed at 25% would result in about $16,667 in taxes, leaving $50,000 after tax.

"It's going to cost you a lot more than you think," Trumbower said.

A taxable withdrawal from retirement savings can also trigger additional costs elsewhere.

For example, if you're receiving Social Security retirement benefits, the added income could cause a larger portion of that money to become taxable. Depending on how much other income you have, 0%, up to 50%, or up to 85% of your Social Security benefits may be subject to federal income tax. A large retirement-account withdrawal can increase that other income and make more of your benefits taxable.

For retirees on Medicare, a large taxable withdrawal also could increase their Part B and Part D premiums through the income-related monthly adjustment amount, or IRMAA.

For 2026, that surcharge applies when modified adjusted gross income exceeds $109,000 for an individual filer or $218,000 for a married couple filing jointly. Medicare generally bases the surcharge on tax information from two years earlier, meaning 2026 premiums are typically based on 2024 income.

"What catches many people off guard is that they often don't feel that impact until a couple of years after the withdrawal," said CFP Ciano Villaquiran, a partner with CURO Financial Planning in San Diego.

For some retirees, it may be worth delaying the additional withdrawal needed to cover the tax bill until the next calendar year, Trumbower said. That second distribution would be taxable in the following year, potentially spreading the income — and its tax impact — across two tax years.

"Pushing the [additional] distribution from the IRA into the following calendar year may possibly avoid jumping up into a higher tax bracket," Trumbower said.

However, she said, retirees should still withhold enough tax from the initial distribution to avoid an underpayment penalty. The federal income tax system is generally pay-as-you-go, meaning taxpayers must pay enough tax through withholding or estimated payments during the year rather than waiting until they file their return.

Generally, taxpayers can avoid an underpayment penalty if their withholding and estimated payments equal at least 90% of the current year's tax liability or 100% of the prior year's tax liability. For certain higher-income taxpayers, the prior-year threshold is 110%. Any remaining tax due generally can be paid when the return is filed.

If you have a brokerage account, that may be a better source to fund a one-off expense, Trumbower said.

Taxes on long-term capital gains — profits on investments such as stocks held for more than one year — are generally lower than ordinary income tax rates. Depending on your taxable income, some long-term capital gains may even be taxed at 0%.

For 2026, the 0% long-term capital gains rate applies to taxable income of up to $49,450 for single filers and $98,900 for married couples filing jointly. Above those thresholds, most long-term capital gains are taxed at 15%, with a 20% rate applying at higher income levels.

Depending on the expense, retirees may be able to borrow money instead of tapping their savings, Trumbower said.

For example, a home equity loan or line of credit could help pay for a major home improvement project, while a car purchase could be financed with an auto loan rather than a large withdrawal from retirement savings.

Of course, borrowing comes with its own costs and risks. The loan payments would need to fit within the retiree's budget, and the interest paid could outweigh the potential benefits of leaving retirement savings untouched.

Additionally, it's worth considering how the withdrawal could affect your savings over time.

"Pulling a large amount out for a one-time expense doesn't just reduce the account balance today; it can reduce what that money could have grown into down the road," Villaquiran said.

For illustration: $50,000 earning 5% annually would grow to about $81,400 after 10 years, according to the Securities and Exchange Commission's Investor.gov compound-interest calculator. If a retiree withdrew $10,000 at the start and left $40,000 invested under the same assumptions, it would grow to about $65,200 — a difference of roughly $16,300 after 10 years.

CNBC receives no compensation from placing financial advisory firms on our Financial Advisor 100 list. Additionally, a firm's or advisor's appearance in our ranking does not constitute an individual endorsement by CNBC of any firm or advisor.

#2818. Robinhood shares fall as crypto slump weighs on profits, offsetting gains in new businesses

Robinhood shares were down around 8% in pre-market trading on Wednesday as investors digested weaker-than-expected third-quarter earnings despite growth in options trading and prediction markets.

Here's how the brokerage performed in its quarter ended Sept. 30, compared with Wall Street estimates, based on a survey of analysts by LSEG:

Robinhood's net income fell to $468 million, or 51 cents per share, from $556 million, or 61 cents per share, a year ago. Revenue represented a 12% increase from $1.27 billion in the same quarter last year.

Despite that gain, transaction-based revenue of $867 million came in below Wall Street estimates from StreetAccount of $879 million, putting focus on whether Robinhood can sustain momentum across its trading businesses and reduce its reliance on volatile market activity.

Robinhood has been diversifying its business as it tries to expand its brokerage app into a broader financial services platform. Options trading and event contracts have become increasingly important sources of transaction revenue, while the company has continued to invest in wealth management, subscriptions and other offerings.

Transaction-based revenue grew 19% from the same period last year to $867 million, driven primarily by a more than 500% increase in event contracts revenue. Additionally, options revenue grew 31% to $399 million and equities revenue rose 41% to $121 million.

Those gains were partially offset by cryptocurrency revenue, which fell 55% to $120 million.

Chief Financial Officer Shiv Verma told CNBC event contracts on crypto movements did well in the quarter, offering investors another way to express a view on crypto despite the slump in prices and spot trading.

Net deposits on the Robinhood platform totaled $15.7 billion for the quarter, equal to a 17% annualized growth rate based on total platform assets at the end of Q2 2026. Over the past year, net deposits totaled $71 billion, representing 21% growth compared with total platform assets at the end of Q3 2025.

#2817. Sean McVay reveals real reason why Rams suddenly changed minds on Puka Nacua’s injury status

Rams receiver Puka Nacua hasn't received a new update on his knee and toe injuries. His recovery and timeline remain the same, the team simply ran out of room on the roster.

After initially deciding against placing their All-Pro wide receiver on injured reserve, the Rams reversed course Thursday night, officially sidelining Nacua for at least four games after he suffered MCL and PCL sprains in his right knee, along with turf toe, on the final offensive play of Sunday's 20-13 victory over the Commanders.

On Friday, Rams coach Sean McVay explained the abrupt change of plans.

Los Angeles Rams wide receiver Puka Nacua warms up before an NFL football game against the Buffalo Bills in Inglewood, Calif., Monday, Oct. 12, 2026. AP Photo/Scott Strazzante

"I'm sure you guys are wondering what changed with Puka. We needed the roster spot," McVay said. "We didn't need it initially."

McVay insisted the decision wasn't prompted by a setback in Nacua's recovery. The Rams never realistically expected their superstar receiver to return before the minimum four-game absence required by injured reserve.

"Some things changed where we needed that spot because of all the moving pieces," McVay said. "That was what we did to be able to get Brennan Presley up."

Nacua, meanwhile, has begun rehabilitation and remains in good spirits, according to McVay.

With Nacua sidelined and fellow star receiver Davante Adams also unavailable, the Rams elevated Presley to the active roster ahead of Sunday's Week 10 rematch against the Cardinals.

Los Angeles Rams head coach Sean McVay talks to reporters following an NFL football game against the Las Vegas Raiders, Sunday, Oct. 25, 2026, in Las Vegas. AP Photo/John Locher

McVay praised Presley's consistency, versatility and ability to contribute beyond the receiving corps.

"He's just one of those guys that makes the plays when his number's called," McVay said. "He's a great teammate, really smart, where he can play a lot of different spots if need be, can contribute on special teams."

Presley could also factor into the kick return rotation alongside Ronnie Rivers and the recently returned Xavier Smith.

For a Rams team whose injury list keeps growing, the decision wasn't particularly complicated. Nacua wasn't coming back anytime soon, and Los Angeles needed another healthy body who could help immediately.
