#2790. Vikings-Packers Final Injury Report

The Minnesota Vikings and Green Bay Packers released their final injury reports, along with injury designations, in advance of their matchup on Sunday at Lambeau Field. Here they are:

PUP = Physically unable to participate list.

IR = Injured reserve. Note: only starters on IR are listed.

Probable = the league defines a Probable injury designation as a 75% or greater chance of playing. Historically, 95% of players designated probable are active and played on game day.

Questionable = the league defines a Questionable injury designation as being 'uncertain' of whether the player will play or not. This replaces the former definition of having a 50% chance of playing. Historically, 70-75% of players with a Questionable injury designation were active and played on game day.

Doubtful = The league defines a Doubtful injury designation to mean the player is unlikely to participate in the game. In practical terms, teams use the designation when a player has less than a 50% chance to suit up, and active game appearances from this designation remain rare. Historically only around 6% of players with a Doubtful designation are active and played on game day.

Out = will not play. Players with this designation are not active on game day and league rules prohibit them from playing.

For the Vikings, kind of the worst-case scenario based on the injury list earlier this week, with only Kyle Van Noy being upgraded today among previous DNP players- the rest of which are out on Sunday, including Blake Cashman, T.J. Hockenson, Tyrion Ingram-Dawkins, Jauan Jennings, and Kyler Murray. Moreover, Jordan Addison and Josh Metellus were added to the injury report as both were limited on Friday and carry questionable injury designations. Hopefully both will be able to go on Sunday, however. Not having Hockenson will make it a bit tougher for the debut of Kirk Cousins on Sunday while not having Blake Cashman means Eric Wilson may have the green dot on defense on Sunday with a greater role for Ivan Pace Jr. and probably Jake Golday as well.

For the Packers, starters Josh Jacobs, Sean Rhyan, and Micah Parsons are out, as expected. Starting linebacker Zaire Franklin is doubtful, meaning he probably won't play on Sunday. A few other starters are questionable for the Packers on Sunday, including right guard Kevin Zeitler, defensive end Lukas Van Ness, and safeties Javon Bullard and Xavier McKinney- who was added to the injury list on Friday with a foot injury. Lastly, running back Chris Brooks is also questionable, and with Josh Jacobs out on Sunday may be expected to have more of a role if he's able to go. But while I expect most of the questionable players to play on Sunday for the Packers, most of them have injuries that may keep them at less than 100%.

Overall, both teams are a bit banged up and will be missing some key starters on Sunday.

#2787. Why failure without Kawhi Leonard is ok, but not without Scottie Barnes

When you start dating, there's something to be said for bad dates and breakups serving as lessons for the do's and don'ts of the world of romance. But, if you keep making the same crummy jokes, not brushing your teeth, over and over again, you're not learning much from those bad dates. At a certain point, losing becomes unproductive.

It's kind of like that with basketball, too. And in the last couple of matches, the Toronto Raptors' losing has been real, real unproductive. Because, it doesn't tell anyone anything that they don't already know.

TORONTO, ON- OCTOBER 21 – Scottie Barnes #4 of the Toronto Raptors and Kawhi Leonard #2 of the Toronto Raptors share a laugh on the bench as the Toronto Raptors play the Chicago Bulls in their season opener at Scotiabank Arena in Toronto. October 21, 2026. Steve Russell/Toronto Star (Steve Russell/Toronto Star via Getty Images) | Toronto Star via Getty Images

Both times when the Raptors have traded for Kawhi Leonard, there's been an implicit gamble attached to that. Leonard won't play a lot of regular season games, but take him to the playoffs okay, and he'll turn into the '84 Terminator, or '85 Jordan, whichever set of skills might be needed more that night.

This season, Kawhi went out early. Okay. That just means that the other guys need to start pulling extra weight a little sooner. And you remember the playoff series against the Cavaliers, and say, "okay, Scottie and RJ, maybe they can anchor this offence, even without a scorer like Brandon Ingram or Kawhi Leonard." And then… Scottie Barnes goes down.

An old teacher of mine told me that "practice doesn't make perfect — perfect practice makes perfect," and it concerns me that that Raptors are just practicing really, far, far away from perfect. Now, Scottie seems like he's going to come back soon; when exactly that will be is unclear, but there has been little indication that the groin strain is something particularly serious.

But, losing the games that the Raptors are losing, without Scottie Barnes, doesn't really help them understand how their team is going to perform in playoff settings, which, for this roster, should be the ultimate goal. Before the season started, Bobby Webster spoke to the media, and argued that the question that should remain pervasive throughout the season should be "can we win every night?"

Thus far, the answer to that question, without Kawhi Leonard, has be a resounding "no." Omer Osman illustrates that clearly, in the tweet above. It's not 2019 anymore. The Raptors are a different beast, younger, less veteran-heavy, and constructed in a less careful manner.

With Scottie missing the last couple of matchups against heavy-hitters like Boston and Philadelphia, the Raptors don't know how well the team can operate in an optimal sans-Kawhi state, because Toronto was never expecting to, and is not built to, compete without both of its All-Stars.

Barnes was showcasing that he's taken a leap offensively, and can piggy-back the Raptors — to a degree — but when facing off against diminished rosters of great teams, and the forward isn't there to help the Raptors take advantage of weakened clubs, the Raptors are effectively leaving both wins or positive reps on the table, and without Barnes, aren't necessarily getting either.

This isn't to say that there's *zero* value from playing without Barnes or Leonard. RJ Barrett just had a season high in points, and is continuing to prove his value. Jakob Poeltl has quietly been looking more like his uninjured self again. And the team's younger players are getting more time on the court to find their legs and develop themselves further. But, this season was never meant to be about development. It was meant to be about the Raptors bashing teams' heads in with lockdown defence and gliding to the playoffs.

But, ultimately, the team has to play the cards they're dealt. The season thus far hasn't been the triumphant return of Raptors basketball to the big stage anyone expected, but there's a lot of basketball left in the year.

@BAAILEYYCREATES on Twitter raised an important point; that last year, Leonard's health management kept him off the court almost entirely until the new year, and when he was back, he was whooping teams left right and centre. It's not 2019 anymore, and Kawhi is 35 years old now, but that means, simply, that ramping up looks different for Leonard now, not that he's unable to deliver on-court product. The Raptors need to be able to compete with and without Leonard for this roster to work, but without Scottie Barnes to lead the charge, Toronto will remain in dire straits.

#2783. What's ahead for AI financing after Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang departs

Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang's departure hasn't produced a broad shock to the credit market, but it is putting the company's role as a financier of artificial intelligence infrastructure under scrutiny.

Huang, who stepped down Thursday amid an espionage and adultery scandal, built Nvidia into an AI powerhouse in the 33 years since he co-founded the company. Chief financial officer Colette Kress has been named interim CEO as the company searches for a permanent chief executive.

Nvidia not only supplies its graphics processing units to hyperscalers and tech giants, it also provides financing to other companies to help the industry expand. That could all change.

"Although we expect the company to fulfill all of its previously announced commitments, we believe that going forward, it may be more difficult for the company to negotiate new multi-party investments among ecosystem partners at the same scale it has done up to this point," Goldman Sachs analyst James Schneider said in a note Thursday.

The news of Huang's resignation comes after investors were already concerned about the massive amount of debt being taken on to fund AI infrastructure.

Tech bond yields have risen and spreads have widened on credit default swaps, most notably in lower-rated names. Credit default swaps are contracts in which investors pay for protection in case the borrower can't repay its debt. Wider spreads indicate investors view the bonds as riskier.

On Friday, the yield on Nvidia's 4.5% bond due in 2031 sat at 5.717%, nearly 5% higher week to date. Bond yields move inversely to prices.

Tech stocks slumped Friday with the Nasdaq 100 closing down 1.2% as markets continued to digest the Nvidia news. Yields on hyperscaler bonds ticked slightly higher on Friday, signaling the broader concern about AI debt lingers.

It's the smaller AI companies that could ultimately see an impact if Nvidia makes any changes to its AI financing. The company had $36 billion in off-balance-sheet AI cloud agreements, or backstops, for neoclouds, according to its last quarterly financial filing in July.

Neoclouds CoreWeave, Nebius and IREN are all in Nvidia's portfolio. Their shares were down sharply Thursday.

CoreWeave, which has become the poster child for AI debt fears, saw its bond yields retreat after its quarterly earnings on Tuesday eased investor concerns. The yield on its 9.75% bond due in 2031 was 14.79% on Friday after climbing to 16.7% last week. The spread on its five-year CDS also dropped more than 17% week to date, although it remained above 1,000 — which is generally considered a sign of distress.

It remains unclear how Nvidia's support for those companies may change, said CreditSights analyst Andy Li.

"We don't know what the ultimate strategy for Nvidia is going to be coming out the other side," he said.

"However, I think directionally, just given the fact that Jensen was so crucial in selling the Nvidia story and being a very aggressive deal maker, it's likely that a lot of this creative deal-making and circular financing and equity and credit support is going to moderate," he added.

CoreWeave CEO Michael Intrator told CNBC Wednesday, before Huang's departure, that Nvidia "is not one person."

"There are thousands of people over there that interface with thousands of people at my company every day. They integrate on the technology. They integrate on the physical infrastructure. They integrate on the networking," he said in an interview with "Squawk on the Street." "They have been a great partner for us for years. They will continue to be a great partner for us."

Bob Lang, founder and chief strategist at Explosive Options, doesn't see any big changes in terms of Nvidia's relevance or its ability to finance for now. As CFO, Kress was involved in the financing deals, and the search for a permanent CEO is going to take some time, he noted.

The real issue is just how much money the company will want to lend for AI financing, rather than, say using its hundreds of billions in free cash flow for other purposes, like share buybacks, capex, or acquisitions.

"Big companies like Nvidia and Meta, they're holding all the cards," he said. "When they decide that the game is over, that's it — it's going to be curtains for some of these smaller companies."

— CNBC'S Kif Leswing and Isabel O'Brien contributed reporting.

#2782. How to watch Warriors vs. Spurs

The Golden State Warriors continue their road trip with Friday night's NBA Cup matchup against the San Antonio Spurs. The game will be played at 6:30 PM PT in San Antonio and can be watched on Prime Video.

Golden State's eight-game winning streak came to an end Wednesday night following a 115-108 loss to the Houston Rockets. However, the result took a backseat to a much bigger concern after superstar Steph Curry suffered an oblique injury in the first quarter that forced him to miss the remainder of the game.

Despite losing their best player early, the Warriors still managed to put up a strong fight against Houston. Rookie Yaxel Lendeborg continued his impressive start to the season, going toe-to-toe with Rockets superstar Kevin Durant at times while leading Golden State with 25 points and 10 rebounds. Brandin Podziemski provided some much-needed scoring support, finishing with 22 points on 7-of-15 shooting from the field, including four three-pointers.

The biggest storyline heading into Friday night is Curry's injury and what it means for Golden State moving forward. The Warriors announced Thursday that their superstar guard suffered an oblique abdominal tear and will be reevaluated in two weeks, leaving the team without its offensive engine for tonight's game and the foreseeable future.

In years past, an extended absence for Curry would have been a devastating blow to Golden State's chances of winning. While that still may be true, the emergence of Lendeborg as a legitimate scoring threat and head coach Steve Kerr's revamped offensive system has this year's Warriors thinking they are much better equipped to weather the storm.

That confidence will be put to the test Friday night against a San Antonio team that enters the matchup with a 10-2 record, the second-best mark in the Western Conference. Wednesday's performance against Houston offered a glimpse of what the Warriors can accomplish without their superstar, but sustaining that level of play will be a tall task, especially against a Spurs team led by reigning Defensive Player of the Year Victor Wembanyama. With Curry sidelined, Golden State will need Lendeborg to build on his impressive rookie campaign, which has seen him average 21.7 points per game on 51.8% shooting from the field, while the team continues its stellar play defensively.

Enjoy the game Dub Nation. GO WARRIORS!!!

Spurs: De'Aaron Fox, Stephon Castle, Dylan Harper, Devin Vassell, Victor Wembanyama

#2777. Is a bottom in for the Nasdaq-100? Charts to watch

(See the video above for Frank's complete chart breakdown.)

Is the Nasdaq-100's recent bounce a sign that the bottom is in? We're going to look at several charts to help answer that question.

First, let's remember how we got here. In September, the NDX broke out from a bullish cup-and-handle pattern, producing a 33,200-upside target. That breakout quickly failed, however, and gave way to a bearish pattern that broke down aggressively two weeks ago.

The decline brought the index relatively close to its 26,350 downside target before the current rebound began.

Zooming out, it's telling to see where the bounce originated. Three important forms of support converged in the 25,000-26,000 area:

This confluence of support helped encourage buying after a roughly 15% decline.

For perspective, we also see what larger drawdowns of 20%, 30%, 40%, and 50% would look like … a lot worse.

The following chart tracks NDX drawdowns from 52-week highs going back to just after the financial crisis. Only a handful of declines exceeded 20%, and three produced relatively quick reversals: late 2018, the Covid lows in 2020 and April 2025.

Historically, buying into declines of approximately 15%–25% has generally paid off, even when additional downside occurred first (yellow highlights).

The chart also highlights previous declines of 10%–16% in green, putting the current pullback into perspective.

The major exception was 2022, when the NDX eventually declined nearly 36% over approximately 10 months ... a much worse outcome, which many investors are concerned could happen again now.

That raises the key question: Are we experiencing another routine correction, or the early stages of a larger bear market?

One important clue will be how far this bounce progresses.

Any rally that fails to reclaim the previous highs could establish a significant lower high. Additional lower highs would provide stronger evidence of a developing, longer downtrend.

We saw this in March 2022, when that first sharp rebound effort failed to reach the previous high before a much more severe decline followed.

The bottom line: The current bounce is encouraging, particularly given where it originated and knowing how well buying prior 10-20% drawdowns has paid off.

From here, it's all about the follow through.  We don't need to see new highs right away, but any rally from here that's met with another wave of strong selling could reignite downside momentum.

Frank Cappelleri is the founder and president of CappThesis, an independent research firm that helps active investors through time-tested chart and statistical analysis. Prior to starting CappThesis, he spent 25 years on Wall Street as an equity sales trader, technical analyst, research sales specialist and desk strategist.

#2772. A trading strategy to ride out the Nvidia uncertainty and generate income in the process

Jensen Huang's abrupt resignation has sent shockwaves through the tech sector, dragging Nvidia's (NVDA) stock down roughly 30% since Oct. 6. The market despises uncertainty, and losing a visionary founder, particularly one without a well-telegraphed succession plan, makes it worse.

However, for opportunistic investors, this panic has made the stock cheaper (obviously) while options have become significantly more expensive. Trading in the $160s, Nvidia's valuation has taken a big haircut, yet its underlying business fundamentals remain largely intact. While Huang was the architect of Nvidia's AI dominance, the company's moat — superior GPUs, the CUDA software ecosystem, and networking expertise — does not walk out the door with him. The company's structural advantage in the global artificial intelligence rollout persists.

Buying good companies at a discount is investing 101, but the current market dynamics may do something else for those who use options.  Huang's departure guarantees that Nvidia will be operating under a cloud of transition for the foreseeable future. Executing a permanent CEO search while navigating global regulatory scrutiny takes time. Consequently, Nvidia's implied volatility (IV)—the market's pricing of expected future price swings — has spiked to highs not seen since the tariff tantrum in the first half of 2025, and this elevated IV is highly likely to persist for several months as the leadership drama slowly unfolds.

This prolonged period of high implied volatility can be a goldmine for option sellers, specifically those executing options overlays such as covered call strategies. When you buy NVDA shares at these depressed levels and simultaneously sell out-of-the-money call options against your position, the premiums you collect are directly tied to IV. Because fear and transition-related uncertainty have dramatically inflated these option prices, the income generated from selling covered calls is now significantly higher than usual.

This lowers the risk profile of holding the stock considerably over time. Consistently collecting these inflated premiums systematically lowers your overall cost basis in the underlying shares. If the stock trades sideways or recovers slowly during the CEO search, you are generating an outsized cash yield on a stagnant asset. If the stock experiences further downside turbulence, the heavy premium income acts as a substantial downside buffer, meaningfully reducing the strategy's overall risk over time.

By deploying a persistent covered call strategy, investors can leverage the exact fear driving the stock down to generate robust, risk-reducing income while maintaining exposure to the undeniable future of AI.

#2769. South Africa vs. Bangladesh 1st Test prediction, lineups and Johannesburg pitch report — who will win?

South Africa vs. Bangladesh 1st Test prediction, lineups and Johannesburg pitch report — who will win? originally appeared on Cricket News. Add Cricket News as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

Barely a fortnight after beating Australia in Cape Town to become the world's number-one Test side, South Africa are back at work against Bangladesh. The 2025-27 World Test Championship cycle rolls on in Johannesburg on November 15.

Bangladesh arrive with barely five days between Tests after Towhid Hridoy's unbeaten 242 rescued a draw with West Indies in Sylhet. They also carry some scars from 2024, when they were bowled out for 159 and 143 in the second Test and lost by an innings and 273 runs.

Their preparations have been hit by injuries. Shadman Islam is out of the series with a tear and a fracture and Mahmudul Hasan Joy has also been ruled out. Uncapped Mahfijul Islam Robin has been called up as a back-up opener, and Mohammad Naim Sheikh returns after a solitary Test in 2022.

Dale Benkenstein has joined as batting specialist.

South Africa have never lost to Bangladesh in 16 Tests, winning 14 and drawing two. None of the last six meetings have been close, with the narrowest a seven-wicket win in 2024 and the rest decided by an innings or by 220 runs or more.

South Africa were effectively 40 for 4 in their second innings in Cape Town, yet still beat Australia by 176 runs, with Simon Harmer taking 10 wickets in the match and Marco Jansen named player of the series. Bangladesh lost 1-0 to West Indies and needed Hridoy's 242 not out to avoid defeat in Sylhet.

Bangladesh do have batters who can make a game of it. Hridoy and Mushfiqur Rahim, the first Bangladesh player to reach 7,000 Test runs, added 135 together against West Indies. But with the opening pair in flux and South Africa's pace attack in rhythm Bangladesh, will struggle for anything.

The Cricket News Prediction: South Africa to win.

Aiden Markram, Marques Ackerman, Tony de Zorzi, Temba Bavuma (c), Matthew Breetzke, Ryan Rickelton (wk), Wiaan Mulder, Marco Jansen, Simon Harmer, Anrich Nortje, Kagiso Rabada

Tanzid Hasan, Mohammad Naim Sheikh, Najmul Hossain Shanto (c), Mominul Haque, Towhid Hridoy, Mushfiqur Rahim, Litton Das (wk), Mehidy Hasan Miraz, Taskin Ahmed, Hasan Mahmud, Shoriful Islam

Teams batting first have won 18 of the 39 Tests here, against 11 for those bowling first, and the average score falls from 311 in the first innings to 204 in the fourth.

#2767. Hugs will wait as Raiders’ ex-Seahawks trio hopes to spoil reunion with former team

For all the time we spend talking about who needs a ring, who deserves a ring, and whose perfectly fine career will forever be held against him because he never won a ring, you'd think a guy who finally got his hands on one would wear the thing to the grocery store.

Andrew Janocko has worn his two times. And only at the urging of his better half.

"I wore it this summer twice at the request of my wife," the Raiders' offensive coordinator said Thursday.

Las Vegas Raiders head coach Klint Kubiak, middle, reacts with assistant coaches during an NFL football game in Santa Clara, Calif., Sunday, Nov. 8, 2026. AP Photo/Justine Willard

The rest of the time, the Super Bowl ring Janocko earned as the Seahawks' quarterbacks coach stays tucked away in a safe deposit box. Which tells you something about how little time the NFL gives coaches to enjoy the very thing they spend their professional lives chasing.

Consider Janocko's boss. On a special Sunday in February, Klint Kubiak called the plays that helped Seattle beat New England for the Super Bowl championsip. The very next day, the Raiders were announcing him as their head coach.

Many of us have had weekends off that lasted far longer than Kubiak got to be the reigning Super Bowl champion offensive coordinator of the Seattle Seahawks.

Kubiak wasted little time bringing two of his Super Bowl lieutenants with him to the desert. He tapped Janocko, who coached Sam Darnold in Seattle, to run the Raiders' offense and Rick Dennison, who coordinated the Seahawks' run game, to coach the offensive line.

Seattle Seahawks head coach Mike Macdonald is doused with Gatorade during Super Bowl LX against the New England Patriots, Sunday, Feb. 8, 2026, in Santa Clara, Calif. Charles Wenzelberg/New York Post

Fernando Mendoza's first NFL start will command most of the attention at Allegiant Stadium, and rightfully so. But consider what the afternoon asks of three men on the Raiders' sideline. They spent last season figuring out how to get a special group of Seahawks to the Super Bowl. They've spent this week trying to make sure their former colleagues have a miserable flight home.

"It's a big one because it's the next one," Janocko said of Sunday's game.

Seattle Seahawks head coach Mike Macdonald watches his players warm up prior to an NFL football game against the Arizona Cardinals in Seattle, Sunday, Nov. 8, 2026. AP Photo/Lindsey Wasson

Give him credit. That might be the only way to make facing the team you won a championship with sound like a Thursday night in Cleveland.

Kubiak had a pretty good reason for holding off on the nostalgia himself. When the subject came up early in the week, he still had a 38–17 loss to the 49ers to sort through. There were five Raiders turnovers on that film. The trip down memory lane could wait until after the walkthrough.

"And I'll turn the page here later this afternoon," he said.

Ask Kubiak what he brought with him from Seattle, though, and he'll give you something to chew on. Tom Brady and the Raiders saw what he did with Darnold and wanted him to develop the quarterback they would select first overall. Kubiak, for his part, was paying attention to how the two men above him, head coach Mike Macdonald and general manager John Schneider, ran a football team.

Fernando Mendoza of the Las Vegas Raiders warms up before a game against the San Francisco 49ers in Santa Clara, California, Sunday, Nov. 8, 2026. Getty Images

"Number one, I think it's just organizationally, just an all-football building, and from John Schneider to Coach Macdonald, just extremely intelligent people that treat people the right way and get the most out of their people because they're genuinely good people."

That might be the piece of Seattle Kubiak can put to work fastest. On Sunday, Mendoza will come off the field after his first series as an NFL starter and look for somebody to talk to. He'll have the head coach who called plays for a Super Bowl winner and the coordinator who sat next to that quarterback between possessions. Whatever the kid just saw, there's a decent chance one of them has seen it across a practice field.

As for what happens when Janocko sees the people he shared all that with, he's made room in the schedule.

"We'll hug and stuff after the game, but we'll let everyone else deal with that."

There'll be time for a handshake, a hug and, if the Raiders can pull this off, maybe a little grief for a few old friends. Anybody hoping to get a look at Janocko's ring, though, ought to take it up with his wife. So far, she's the only one who's gotten him to wear it.

#2765. Predictions: Lions vs. New England Patriots

Nolan Bianchi, Richard Silva, John Niyo and Bob Wojnowski of The Detroit News offer predictions for Sunday's game between the Lions and New England Patriots at Allianz Arena in Munich, Germany (9:30 a.m., Fox/97.1 FM).

▶ Nolan Bianchi: The Patriots are coming off a whupping at the hands of the Packers, which is never what an upcoming opponent wants to see. New England still has a ton of leftover talent from last year's Super Bowl run but hasn't been able to consistently put the pieces together this season. Still, their defensive front could give Detroit problems, considering how much the Lions have struggled to protect Jared Goff in recent weeks. But I suspect the Lions' defense could continue its run against a shorthanded Patriots receiving corps. And for as much as the Patriots' defense wants to rebound from last week's 45-point outpouring, the Lions' offense will be looking to get back on track, too, after scoring 20 points or less in three straight games. Pick: Lions, 28-23

▶ John Niyo: The Patriots got a taste of the NFC North last week and didn't like it at all. But the Lions need to realize this might be the second-best offense they've faced to date. Keeping Drake Maye in the pocket will be the key for the Lions' defense getting off the field on third downs. But injuries at running back may help them apply more pressure and force Maye into the kind of poor decisions that really cost him last week against Green Bay. On the other side of the ball, the Lions' offensive line needs a bounce-back performance in a big way. The Patriots' secondary looks vulnerable right now, but there's real talent back there, as well as a front that's capable of shutting down Jahmyr Gibbs and the run game. Pick: Lions, 27-24

▶ Richard Silva: This'll be a nice test for Detroit's defense, which allowed 31.8 points per game from Weeks 1-4 but has since rebounded well; the Lions have allowed an average of 15.5 points over their last four contests. The Patriots haven't been very consistent, but they rank 10th in Expected Points Added (EPA) per play this season, the second-highest ranking among all of the teams the Lions have played thus far (the Buffalo Bills, who played the Lions in Week 2, are the exception). Put simply: The Patriots are, statistically, among the better offenses the Lions will face, and a positive performance against them would further legitimize this defensive turnaround. I don't think traveling to Germany will have any more of an impact on the Lions than it will the Patriots. Any effects, at least in my opinion, would show themselves in Week 11, when the Lions come back to Ford Field to host the Tampa Bay Buccaneers, who will be coming off a bye week. But that's a problem for next week. Pick: Lions, 28-24

▶ Bob Wojnowski: The Lions have played considerably better than the Patriots of late, which usually means one thing in the NFL: Watch out for the bounce-back. New England (5-4) has lost two of three and was embarrassed at home by the Packers 45-27, with Drake Maye now tied for the league lead with 11 interceptions. They'll be an ornery bunch. The Lions (6-2) have won four straight with an improving defense, but other than their 20-17 victory over Green Bay and Jordan Love, haven't faced a top-notch quarterback. The Patriots haven't resembled their Super Bowl team but they're expected to get star receiver A.J. Brown back from injury. The Lions' offense has hit a rare three-game sputter, and Jahmyr Gibbs could face tough sledding against a tough run defense. Brad Holmes' questionable decision to basically stand pat at the trade deadline will be judged quickly. Especially if Maye finds his groove in Germany and turns the Lions' secondary into sauerkraut. Pick: Patriots, 31-27

#2762. Pelicans star Dejounte Murray requests trade out of New Orleans

Dejounte Murray has had enough of New Orleans.

The former All-Star requested a trade from the Pelicans, with his agent, Klutch Sports CEO Rich Paul, and team officials beginning to work toward finding him a new home, ESPN's Shams Charania first reported.

The Pelicans then put out a statement and said they are looking into "potential trade options."

"Today, Dejounte Murray's representatives informed us of his request to be traded," the Pelicans said in a statement shared on X. "We will evaluate potential trade opportunities that are in the best interest of our organization."

The trade request comes less than two days after Murray took to social media to suggest there was "sabotage" going on behind the scenes with the team.

After the Pelicans' blowout 145-111 home loss to the Nets on Wednesday, Murray took to X, pointing out that the team was 1-8 with him in the lineup this season and 3-0 without him.

Dejounte Murray of the New Orleans Pelicans reacts during a game against the Golden State Warriors in San Francisco, Monday, Nov. 9, 2026. Getty Images

"From what's going on INSIDE, this sounds like someone hit you to say this!! THE 'SABOTAGE' IS REAL!!" Murray wrote in the since-deleted post.

Murray, 30, reposted Charania's report on Friday afternoon.

At least one potential suitor has already emerged.

The Suns have expressed interest in acquiring Murray, per NBA insider Chris Haynes.

Murray has had a disastrous tenure with the franchise since he was traded by the Hawks in July 2024 for a package headlined by Dyson Daniels and two first-round picks.

Dejounte Murray and Derik Queen of the New Orleans Pelicans high-five during a game against the Brooklyn Nets in New Orleans, Wednesday, Nov. 11, 2026. NBAE via Getty Images

Daniels has since emerged as one of the best perimeter defenders in the NBA, while Murray has appeared in just 54 games in a Pelicans uniform, suffering a fractured hand in his New Orleans debut before tearing his Achilles in January 2025.

The Pelicans, who hired Jamahl Mosley as their head coach during the offseason, have lost four straight games, including a 51-point drubbing at the hands of the Warriors on Monday.

Murray is making $32.8 million this season and holds a $30.8 million player option for the 2027-28 campaign after signing a $114-plus million veteran extension in 2023.

#2760. Pair of 49ers stars suddenly questionable for Cowboys game after practice injuries

Friday the 13th came early for the 49ers.

Coach Kyle Shanahan revealed that two of the Niners' most important players were suddenly questionable for their showdown against the Cowboys this Sunday.

The 49ers could be without All-Pro left tackle Trent Williams and stalwart linebacker Dre Greenlaw after both players suffered injuries in practice this week.

Williams tweaked his shoulder Thursday, and Greenlaw hurt his knee in the same session. Williams sat out Friday's practice, while Greenlaw was listed as a limited participant.

Shanahan kept his cards close to the vest about either player's status for Sunday.

San Francisco 49ers head coach Kyle Shanahan answers a question during a news conference after an NFL football game in Atlanta, Sunday, Oct. 25, 2026. AP Photo/Brynn Anderson

"It just means that he's questionable, whatever that means," Shanahan said. "Anytime you hurt your shoulder on a Thursday, it doesn't make you totally at ease. Sunday comes fast. But hopefully that's enough time for him to be all right."

Brock Purdy has been sacked only eight times this season, but if Williams, 38, isn't able to go, he could have a gaping hole on his blindside. Led by Quinnen Williams, the Cowboys have racked up 12 sacks over their past four games, getting to the quarterback at least twice in each.

Trent Williams (71) of the San Francisco 49ers gets set against the Las Vegas Raiders on Sunday, Nov. 8, 2026, at Levi's Stadium in Santa Clara, California. Getty Images

Williams' absence would be "huge," Shanahan said.

"The left tackle in general is always huge, and then a guy of Trent's caliber is huge," Shanahan said. "He's a captain for a reason and everyone knows Trent Williams is one of the best players in this league."

Veteran backup Vederian Lowe would likely step in for Williams. Enrique Cruz, a second-year seventh-round pick, would then be potentially one snap away from seeing his first live action.

Had it happened earlier in the week, Shanahan said the 49ers would have considered activating Austen Pleasants. The fourth-year lineman has been on injured reserve since the start of the season but "could" have been an option if he had been able to practice, Shanahan said.

"Vederian's been doing great," Shanahan said. "We've had Cruz working, so we've got the numbers there to handle it."

Williams, in his 16th season, has been playing at as high of a level as ever. He has also been durable, playing every competitive snap so far this season.

Dre Greenlaw (57) of the San Francisco 49ers tackles Ashton Jeanty (2) of the Las Vegas Raiders on Sunday, Nov. 8, 2026, at Levi's Stadium in Santa Clara, Calif. Getty Images

The 49ers, on the other hand, already have experience playing without Greenlaw. When he was suspended for their Monday night win over the Commanders, rookie Jaden Dugger filled in and played well.

He managed to make an impact last week against the Raiders despite getting on the field for only 12 snaps, picking off Kirk Cousins and taking it to the house on the first play of the second half.

Shanahan said that "hopefully" the 49ers would have Greenlaw on Sunday.

"But if we don't," he said, "next man up. And our next man's proven himself so far."

#2757. Troy Aikman engages in potential pre-tampering with Kyle Shanahan

If Troy Aikman exits broadcasting after this season (last month, he put the chance at “50-50”) and if Aikman lands a key position in a front office, the name at the top of his head-coaching wishlist will already be known.

Via Brandon Contes of Awful Announcing, Aikman said during a Thursday appearance on 96.7 The Ticket in Dallas that, if he were running an NFL team, he would try to hire 49ers coach Kyle Shanahan.

“I’ve been asked a lot of questions here, certainly over the last several weeks, about any aspirations that I may have of going in a front office,” Aikman said. “Or ‘Who would you hire as your head coach?’ If I was in a position to hire someone as a head coach, [the] first call I’d make is to the 49ers and say, ‘How much is it going to take to get Kyle Shanahan? Because I think he’s that good. And yes, I’ve always believed this, but coaching matters and it has an impact on an organization.”

It’s not tampering because Aikman currently isn’t running a team. (It could technically be “tampering light” if he’s still working for the Dolphins.) And the information gives Shanahan plenty of leverage in his dealings with the 49ers, if Aikman joins the likes of Matt Millen, John Lynch, and John Elway as former players with little or no front-office experience who find themselves working in high-level jobs with a team.

Aikman has previously made similar comments about Shanahan, in the context of the Bears’ latest coaching vacancy.

“There has been discussion about, ‘Do you give up some picks and see if you can maybe make a deal for a guy like Kyle Shanahan?’” Aikman said during a game in 2024. “I would be all in on that. Because he would develop a quarterback, and he’s a hell of a head coach. But can you pull that off? That’s a better question. And who might you hire that has no head-coaching experience as an offensive guy? You never know the answer to that, as to how good they’re gonna be.”

While it seems to be working out pretty well for the Bears and their head coach who had no head-coaching experience, the point remains that Aikman would try to hire Shanahan, if Aikman is ever in a position to hire a coach.

And there’s one last twist to this story that currently isn’t known. When Aikman was consulting with the Dolphins during their latest search for a G.M. and head coach, did Aikman advise Miami to call about Shanahan? If so, did they?

Shanahan is in his 10th season with the 49ers. He’s taken the team to two Super Bowls. Barring the franchise’s first championship since 1994 (which could come this year), there could be a point where Shanahan reaches an Andy Reid-style mutual expiration date.

If that ever happens, Shanahan will have options. Especially if Aikman ends up getting hired to be someone’s G.M.

#2755. FAA flight debacle could disrupt Rams, Chargers, 49ers; NFL responds

The Rams have spent all week preparing for the Cardinals. Now, an unprecedented travel nightmare could force them to prepare for a road trip of an entirely different kind.

A nationwide ground stop issued by the Federal Aviation Administration on Friday afternoon has thrown weekend travel plans into chaos, potentially affecting several NFL teams scheduled to fly to their respective games Saturday.

U.S. Transportation Secretary Sean Duffy announced the shutdown about 1:15 p.m. PT, citing a communications issue affecting air traffic control centers across the country.

The grounding, reportedly only the second nationwide stoppage since the Sept. 11 terrorist attacks, has already disrupted college football travel. If it stretches into Saturday, the NFL could be next.

The Rams are scheduled to fly to Arizona on Saturday afternoon ahead of Sunday's 1:05 p.m. PT kickoff against the Cardinals in Glendale.

Los Angeles Rams defensive end Aaron Donald walks from the team bus. Los Angeles Times via Getty Imag

If the FAA's grounding order remains in effect throughout Saturday, Los Angeles could be forced to make the approximately 390-mile journey by charter bus, a six-to-seven-hour drive under favorable traffic conditions.

No contingency plan has been announced by the Rams, and there is no indication their scheduled departure will be affected.

Passengers check departure boards at Logan International Airport in East Boston, Mass., Friday, Nov. 13, 2026, during an FAA national ground stop. REUTERS

But with kickoff less than 48 hours away, the possibility of loading an entire NFL roster, coaching staff and equipment onto buses is hardly ideal.

FAA nationwide ground stop live updates: Agency lifts nationwide ground stop after flights halted for 4 hours

JetBlue cancels most remaining flights due to FAA ground stop — and predicts chaos won't end soon

FAA launched new AI 'SMART' tool to all air route traffic control centers just days before nationwide ground stop

College football flight chaos ensues after rare nationwide FAA ground stop

The 49ers are scheduled to fly to Dallas on Saturday, while the Chargers must travel across the country to Baltimore for Monday night's matchup against the Ravens.

The Raiders are home against Seattle, but the Seahawks could face travel complications.

Other teams have more manageable alternatives. Pittsburgh to Cincinnati, Minnesota to Green Bay and Buffalo to New Jersey (for Jets game) are all drives of roughly 300 miles or less.

NFL commissioner Roger Goodell arrives for an NFL game between the Pittsburgh Steelers and the New Orleans Saints in Saint-Denis, France, Sunday, Oct. 25, 2026. AFP via Getty Images

For teams traveling across multiple time zones, however, there is no convenient backup plan.

The NFL has not announced any schedule changes, and the situation remains entirely dependent on how quickly the FAA restores normal operations.

"It's too early. We could address the issue at the appropriate time, if necessary," said a league spokesman when asked about the ground stop.

For now, the Rams are preparing to fly. Whether they end up taking Interstate 10 instead could depend on what happens in the next 24 hours.

#2754. Walker Kessler wants to improve one lesser-known aspect of his game

Nearly a month into the NBA season, Walker Kessler is largely looking like he was well worth the price the Los Angeles Lakers paid to obtain him in July. They gave up two first-round draft picks and two pick swaps in a sign-and-trade deal with the Utah Jazz, then signed him to a four-year contract worth $130 million.

Plenty of pundits felt, and possibly still feel, it was a steep price to pay. But Kessler is averaging 14.5 points on 67.5% field-goal shooting, 10.8 rebounds, 2.2 blocks and 1.2 steals a game, and the 7-foot-2 center is doing an admirable job of anchoring the paint on both ends of the floor.

He is also averaging 1.8 assists a game, and he has posted at least three assists in each of his last three games. Kessler isn't known for his passing or facilitating skills, but based on comments he made after Wednesday's 123-112 win over the Milwaukee Bucks, he wants to improve in that area, via Lakers Nation.

"I gotta do a lot better job I can tell you that," Kessler said. "I'm getting the ball stripped from me too much when I hold the ball low. There's a lot of factors, but yeah, I gotta do a better job of that. But yeah, it's kind of a game feel. If you feel like you can go up with it depending on who's around you, if you pass it out. It just kinda depends."

The Lakers utilize Kessler in a lot of pick-and-roll sequences, and when he hits teammates off the short roll in those plays, it helps their offense function more smoothly. It would also come in handy if Kessler could find open teammates out of the post, whether he's stationed deep in the paint or closer to the elbow.

Right now, he's functioning more as a finisher. He's doing a good job of getting open near the rim, getting passes from teammates and finishing. Of course, on the defensive end, he's making it difficult for opposing teams to score in the paint. As much as he's been blocking shots, he's been changing many more shots, and he's also been preventing opposing players from penetrating all the way to the hoop.

Thanks in part to Kessler's play, the Lakers are 11-2 and have a seven-game winning streak. They will complete a four-game road trip this Sunday when they face the Phoenix Suns.

#2751. NFL predictions: Can the Falcons slow down Kenneth Walker?

The Falcons had a rough outing in Week 9, falling by two scores to the Cincinnati Bengals in Madrid. Atlanta was able to compete for the first part of the game, but quickly fell behind and ultimately looked outclassed by one of the NFL's premier offenses. That result, and the way it unfolded, seemed to prompt some soul-searching from the Falcons coaching staff and front office. In the ensuing days, Atlanta traded away star safety Jessie Bates III and starting center Ryan Neuzil.

That seems to signal a change in philosophy for the Falcons to more prominently focusing on the future. Atlanta isn't going to tank or anything close to it—the NFC South is led by a 3-win team at the moment, so nobody is even close to being "out of it". But this is at least a tacit acknowledgement that the current construction of the team isn't close enough to turn down future assets. It's the right move, and an important realization for the new staff.

That said, there's still a big game coming up this weekend against the Kansas City Chiefs in Week 10. The Falcons are currently 3.5-point home underdogs, per FanDuel, and there are some significant injury question marks to consider heading into this one.

The biggest matchup to watch here will be the beleaguered Falcons run defense against the electric Chiefs rushing attack headed by former Seahawks star Kenneth Walker. Walker has been a godsend for Kansas City, finding a new level of success beyond even his final Super Bowl-winning season in Seattle. With the Chiefs passing game still a bit lackluster, Walker's dominance on the ground has been a key cog in their offensive machine.

Even though Atlanta started out the season with fantastic run defense, teams have largely figured out how to beat the Falcons attacking-style front with inside runs and a lot of man/gap concepts. The Falcons haven't yet developed a counterpunch, and until they do…it's going to be a long day for the defense against any team with even a semi-competent rushing attack. Slowing down Kenneth Walker—or having him not play, as he's currently questionable for the game—is a pivotal matchup to watch.

On the other side of the ball, the Falcons will have to build some offensive consistency. Michael Penix Jr. is in the midst of a two-game slump and was unable to find much success against a fairly beatable Bengals defense. The bigger issue might be the ground game, which should be the backbone of the offense but hasn't had any consistent success in over a month. With Ryan Neuzil heading out and Corey Levin taking over at center, I'm not expecting significant strides there. But perhaps Levin is a better fit for what Bill Callahan wants to do, given their experience together.

This is a tough matchup for Atlanta against a Super Bowl-contending Chiefs team. I'm not expecting much from the offense or defense, to be completely honest. With a bye coming up in Week 11, I worry we might see a "let's just get to the bye" performance. Hopefully I'm wrong, and the Falcons can at least give us an entertaining game on Sunday. An upset victory would keep Atlanta right in the thick of things in the NFC South, and the latter part of the schedule is considerably easier than the last month and a half. We'll see if the Falcons can do it.

#2750. Cam Skattebo stayed in his stance instead of pushing Jameis Winston's tush on key play

Giants running back Cam Skattebo stayed in his stance and didn’t participate in the play on a key short-yardage snap late in the Giants’ loss on Thursday night.

With the Giants leading 21-17 and 3:56 remaining in the fourth quarter on Thursday night, they faced a third-and-1 and sent Jameis Winston in for a quarterback sneak. A first down would have given the Giants a great chance at a long, clock-draining drive. Instead they were stopped, punted, and ended up losing.

As Winston plowed into the line, Skattebo was behind him and could have run in to try to push him forward to gain a yard. Instead, Skattebo stayed in his stance, and by the time he actually got involved in the play, the Commanders had stopped Winston’s forward progress.

It’s unclear why Skattebo didn’t do anything, and he wasn’t asked about it after the game. But at a time when every team in the league is well aware of the value of teammates pushing the quarterback forward on sneaks, it was a bizarre play, and one that proved costly for the Giants.

#2748. Las Vegas Raiders-Seattle Seahawks FEED Topic: Monitoring Fernando Mendoza’s 1st start

While Kirk Cousins exceeded expectations as the Las Vegas Raiders' starting quarterback, getting the team to a 4-5 record and in the playoff mix at the halfway point of the season, Week 10 against the Seattle Seahawks will be the official start of the Fernando Mendoza era in Las Vegas.

John Spytek, Klint Kubiak and the Raiders' coaching staff determined that the rookie is ready for action, trading Cousins to the Minnesota Vikings for a third-round pick and naming Mendoza the starter. That creates plenty of excitement throughout the organization and fanbase. However, the No. 1 pick's first start couldn't have come against a much tougher opponent.

Seahawks head coach Mike Macdonald has a reputation as a "defensive mastermind", running a scheme that has a lot of moving parts and can create confusion for opposing quarterbacks, both from a pressure and coverage standpoint.

For example, Seattle's defense ranks first in yards per game allowed (244.2), tied for fourth in points per game (18.0), tied for third in EPA per pass (-0.17) and 13th in pressure rate (39.6%), according to Sumer Sports, this season. Also, Macdonald is 6-0 during the regular season against rookie quarterbacks, partly because even veteran signal callers can have a tough time deciphering his defense.

But the 2025 Heisman Trophy winner will have some help. Kubiak should be able to prepare him for what type of looks he'll face on Sunday since Kubiak worked for Macdonald last year as the Seahawks' offensive coordinator. Additionally, having a unique offensive weapon who can bail quarterbacks out in Brock Bowers, as well as quality check-down options in Michael Mayer and Ashton Jeanty, gives Mendoza a few security blankets to get out of trouble.

There's probably going to be some ups and downs on Sunday — and likely for the rest of the season, for that matter. But if the rookie looks sharp against arguably the best defense in the league this weekend, that should give him a lot of confidence moving forward and bode well for the Raiders' future.

So, for this week's game day discussion on The FEED, we'll be keeping tabs on how Mendoza looks in his first start. The game kicks off at 1:05 p.m. PST and will air on CBS (check your local listings). I look forward to talkin' some ball with y'all!

#2747. Isaksen: “Non nego che ora è un piacere raggiungere il campo di allenamento”

Dall'infortunio alla rinascita con la Lazio: l'esterno danese incorona Gattuso e racconta il suo momento magico tra campo e spogliatoio.

La sosta di novembre vede la Lazio capolista in solitaria in campionato, con un punto di vantaggio sui campioni in carica. Tra i grandi protagonisti di questo avvio di stagione biancoceleste c'è sicuramente l’esterno danese Gustav Isaksen: attuale capocannoniere della Lazio con 4 reti (al pari di Frattesi e Noslin) e fresco erede della fascia da capitano vista l'assenza per infortunio di Zaccagni. Appena arrivato nel ritiro della Danimarca, l‘esterno ha raccontato ai microfoni di sport.tv2.dk il suo momento di grazia e le straordinarie doti tattiche e umane di mister Gattuso.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Ora sono davvero felice, ho trovato la pace interiore sia dentro che fuori dal campo e acquisito più fiducia in me stesso dopo un’estate complessa, tra operazioni e riabilitazione. Il corpo sta rispondendo al meglio e sto riuscendo a dare il mio 100%. Adesso ho un tecnico che vede qualcosa di grande in me e crede nelle mie potenzialità, con Gattuso non devo pensare troppo. Con i precedenti allenatori, anche molto preparati e comunque importanti per la mia crescita, il mio compito era prettamente tattico e difensivo. Ora, invece, devo soltanto seguire il mio avversario in copertura. La nostra strategia mira a difendere in modo da poter subito ribaltare l’azione e attaccare, è questo il modo in cui amo giocare da sempre.

Il mister è un eccellente motivatore, la sua qualità più importante è la capacità di riuscire a tirare fuori il meglio da ogni singolo calciatore. Non nego che ora è un piacere raggiungere il campo di allenamento, nello spogliatoio non vediamo l’ora. Abbiamo tanto riposo, ma quando ci alleniamo è durissima. Si corre come dei pazzi, i ritmi sono elevati e una volta tornati a casa abbiamo tutti bisogno di riposare e dormire. Ma il gruppo funziona alla perfezione ed è fantastico. In questo momento ho fatto il recupero e mi sento bene.

#2740. Philip Rivers named NC State head coach

NC State has made it official: Philip Rivers is the football program’s new head coach.

After word emerged Rivers was the frontrunner to land the job, his alma mater announced his hiring on Friday.

“I am humbled and honored to be the next head football coach at NC State University,” Rivers said in a statement. “In many ways, I feel like I’m coming home. This is where it all began for me as an 18-year-old kid from Alabama, and now to return to this great place as head coach is beyond words.

“I gave it everything I had here as a player for 51 games and I will give it everything I have as the coach. There’s no denying that this is going to take hard work, but we’re not scared of hard. I look forward to getting after it every single day with an unbelievable coaching staff and a group of players who are dedicated to relentlessly pursuing victory.”

Rivers, who came out of retirement to play for the Colts down the stretch last year, became a high school coach in Alabama in 2021. This will be his first coaching job at the college or pro level, though he interviewed for the Bills’ vacancy in the offseason.

Rivers replaces longtime coach Dave Doeren, who was fired in October. He had been with the Wolfpack since 2013.

#2738. What channel is Washington vs. Michigan State today? Time, TV schedule

Michigan State's home game against Washington on Saturday, Nov. 14, will kick off later than originally scheduled after a nationwide Federal Aviation Administration ground stop disrupted the Huskies' travel plans Friday.

The game, originally scheduled for 4:15 p.m., will instead begin at 5:30 p.m.

Washington's football team was at the airport preparing to depart for East Lansing when the FAA ordered a nationwide ground stop. The Huskies returned to campus before making plans to resume travel Friday evening after the order was lifted.

"With the FAA lifting the nationwide ground stop, the Washington football team anticipates resuming travel to Michigan State this evening," the team said in a statement posted to X.

More: MSU's Burt, Boyd earn Big Ten Player of the Week for win over Michigan

Michigan State athletics said Friday night it was in contact with Washington and the Big Ten and would continue monitoring the situation.

The Spartans (3-6, 1-6 Big Ten) are coming off a 15-6 victory over Michigan that snapped a six-game losing streak and returned the Paul Bunyan Trophy to East Lansing. Washington (5-4, 2-4) is coming off a 28-25 loss to Penn State.

More: Rivalry win opens Michigan State's path to bowl game: 'Bigger things to conquer'

Here's how to watch Washington vs. Michigan State in Week 11 of the college football season:

Radio: WJR 760 AM in Detroit; Spartan Media Network

Chris Vosters will handle play-by-play for Big Ten Network, with Rhett Lewis as analyst and Caroline Hendershot reporting from the sidelines.

George Blaha will have the Michigan State radio call alongside analyst Jehuu Caulcrick and sideline reporter Tre Mosley.

Records: Washington 5-4, Michigan State 3-6

Saturday marks the sixth meeting between Michigan State and Washington and the first since the Huskies joined the Big Ten.

Washington has won four consecutive meetings, including a 41-7 victory in East Lansing in 2023. The teams have split their previous two games at Spartan Stadium.

Moneyline: Washington -160, Michigan State +142

Washington enters as a narrow favorite. Betting lines are subject to change.

#2737. Robert Griffin III is "expected to interview" for Baylor job

Other former players who have become college head coaches include Deion Sanders, Eddie George, and Mike Vick.

Getting an interview and getting the job are obviously two very different things. Still, Griffin has a foot in the door. Without that, it’s impossible to get an invitation to the table.

#2735. John Harbaugh knew about the Jameis Winston "who let the dogs out?" cadence

Thursday night’s game between the Commanders and the Giants introduced a new wrinkle to the legend of Jameis Winston.

While attempting to get the Washington defense to jump offside on a fourth-and-one play in the fourth quarter, Jameis capped his pre-snap call with, “Who let the dogs out?”

The move invited curiosity as to whether Jameis was simply being Jameis and seizing on the moment, or whether coach John Harbaugh knew that the homage to the Baha Men was part of the plan.

“Yeah, I knew that,” Harbaugh told reporters. “That was part of the cadence, yeah. That’s part of the cadence. You know, teams do jump in those situations sometimes. So that was the idea.”

He’s right. It seems like at least once a week a defense jumps when it knows it shouldn’t. And it doesn’t take much to spark the desired reaction.

Last night, it’s possible that the specific cadence could have prompted one of the defenders to start laughing loud enough to tumble forward in his stance.

Regardless, it was fun. And that’s when football is at its best — when it’s fun.

#2734. Lakers stars Luka Doncic, Austin Reaves earn different status for battle with Suns

The Lakers received positive injury news following multiple scares in Wednesday's 123-112 win over the Bucks.

Lakers coach JJ Redick said Austin Reaves suffered a left heel bruise in the third quarter of that game and is listed as day-to-day ahead of Sunday's contest against the Suns.

The Lakers' medical staff and Reaves jointly determined that he didn't need further imaging after seeing how he felt Thursday morning. Reaves had X-rays Wednesday evening and they came back clean.

When asked if Reaves' injury was minor, Redick didn't hesitate.

"I would assume a day-to-day injury is minor, yeah," Redick said.

Austin Reaves #15 of the Los Angeles Lakers shoots a free throw against the Milwaukee Bucks on Wednesday, Nov. 11, 2026, at Fiserv Forum in Milwaukee. NBAE via Getty Images

As for Luka Doncic, who tweaked his right ankle in the fourth quarter of Wednesday's game?

"Luka is fine," Redick said. "I think the expectation would be he's available."

Collin Sexton is also listed as day-to-day after receiving multiple stitches to close a deep cut over his right eye after he and Bucks guard Ryan Rollins bumped heads in the fourth quarter.

Redick said a concussion was ruled out for Sexton.

Meanwhile, Quentin Grimes, who missed the Lakers' last two games because of a sprained right pinkie, participated in the live portion of the "stay ready game" at Friday's practice.

Los Angeles Lakers' Collin Sexton (10) dribbles against Milwaukee Bucks' Ryan Rollins (13) during a game in Milwaukee on Wednesday, Nov. 11, 2026. Getty Images

Grimes, who had his pinkie taped, said the swelling has gone down and his finger is "basically back to normal."

"I definitely expect to play, for sure," Grimes said of Sunday.

It's all positive news for the Lakers, who have won seven straight games and have the best record in the league at 11-2.

While Doncic spoke after Wednesday's game saying his ankle was "painful a little bit but I'll be fine," there was significantly more worry around Reaves, who did not speak to the media after the Lakers' win and was expected to receive more imaging upon the team's return to Los Angeles — until it was deemed unnecessary.

When Redick was asked if Doncic advised Reaves about the heel injury that he suffered in 2024, the coach said he wasn't sure.

Los Angeles Lakers' Luka Doncic looks at his ankle during an NBA basketball game against the Milwaukee Bucks in Milwaukee, Wednesday, Nov. 11, 2026. AP Photo/Aaron Gash

"I know those guys text 47 times a day, so maybe there's a chance," Redick said. "Austin said he woke up yesterday at 7 a.m. and he had four texts from Luka, so maybe one of those was the heel text. I don't know."

Following a brutal stretch of three road games in four days, including a back-to-back, the Lakers had a much-needed three days to regroup before playing the Suns.

Redick and his staff spent the last three days auditing the Lakers, who are the top-rated team in the league on offense but are No. 18 on defense.

His analytics team prepared a 21-page presentation.

"We know we want to be a top-10 defense," Redick said. "We know we are not going to be able to do that unless we become better than a bottom-10 transition team and better than a bottom 10-defensive rebounding team."

#2733. On Kirk Cousins (and Packers Week, Too!)

Kirk Cousins is once again the quarterback for the Minnesota Vikings. Welcome to the most exciting, if anxiety-riddled, stretch of Vikings football in memory. The next few months will be unlike any other.

General manager Nolan Teasley did what he had to do. A 3rd-round pick was a small price to pay to avoid the iceberg. After an embarrassing, mind-boggling, inept offensive showing in front of the entire nation on Monday Night Football that threatened a full-on locker room mutiny and the season's implosion, the ship has now been steered into calmer waters. Whether it will be there when the calendar turns to January is still very much up in the air.

The only situations even remotely comparable were Sam Bradford's arrival days before the 2016 season and Randy Moss' return in 2010. While the former started well, defeating the Green Bay Packers in Week 1, it ultimately petered out. The latter didn't even get that far. Let's hope for better luck this time around.

Super Bowl Dreams Are Back On. Through nine games as the Raiders' starter, with offensive weapons no one would confuse with the 1999 Rams, Kirk Cousins was on pace for more TD passes (38) than he had with the Vikings (2021 and 2022) after the NFL moved to a 17-game regular season. He would just fall short of the passing yardage he had in 2022 (4,547).

In an earlier article, I noted that a torn Achilles in a quarterback's plant leg is the worst non-throwing-arm injury you can sustain. Recovery is challenging and often takes more than one season before you start to look like yourself again. I think that may explain much of what's going on with Daniel Jones in Indy right now.

Given his performance this year, there is a persuasive argument that the nature of the injury, not necessarily age, may explain the inconsistency Cousins experienced during his stint with the Atlanta Falcons. Would he have played better if he were 25? Probably. But his play for the Raiders has been a pleasant surprise that no one envisioned before the season: 2nd in passing TDs, 5th in yards, 9th in EPA per dropback, etc.

All Cousins needs to do is be good. That's it. The Raiders are 8th in points per game (26.4). We won't even need that 95% of the time. We'd be 8-0 right now. The reason the Silver and Black are 4-5 is that their defense is last in points allowed (29.8) and yards allowed (385.4). They're basically the 2020-21 Vikings. Under Brian Flores, we're 1st and 2nd in those categories, respectively.

The NFC is wide open right now. No one is convincing me that there is a team we can't beat, with Cousins back throwing to Justin Jefferson, Jordan Addison, and T.J. Hockenson again, and Flores' defense as the ultimate insurance policy.

Cousins, Storybook Ending? Hollywood's most gifted screenwriters couldn't come up with a better scenario. Cousins returns to the Vikings to save the season, and his first game is at Lambeau Field – the very place his Vikings tenure ended the first time around. All under the larger theme of redemption, given how those highly debated years ended after the 2023 season.

Cousins realizes the feel-good, storybook ending is within reach. Reports say the Raiders left the trade decision in his hands. He knows. This is his last, best chance. A Kirk Cousins with a Super Bowl ring would not only have a legitimate Hall of Fame resume (the numbers will be there), but he'd also have a statue outside U.S. Stadium to immortalize his role in ending the long-suffering anguish of a tortured fan base.

Patience is a virtue. I'd urge people not to expect Cousins to come in seamlessly and pick up where he left off in 2023. It may take a game or two to get back into a rhythm with the offense and receivers, regardless of previous experience. That certainly doesn't mean we won't win on Sunday. We've already beaten the Packers and have won games this season with an offense that made us long for the days of the Wishbone or Single-Wing. This is about ensuring the offense is hitting its stride heading into December and (hopefully) a memorable playoff run.

KOC Gets a Mulligan (For Now). Cousins' return effectively gives KOC a new lease on life after repeatedly stepping on rakes with disastrous quarterback decisions that necessitated the trade. He may have gotten away with the "don't look at me!" routine in drafting J.J. McCarthy and letting Sam Darnold walk, but the 2026 quarterback room mess is on him. You can only play that hand so many times. Kwesi Adofo-Mensah took the blame for the earlier missteps and lost his job. Nolan Teasley can justifiably plead ignorance for being late to the game, and no one believes he traded McCarthy without KOC's approval (or encouragement).

Regardless of how this season ends, the Vikings will again begin the offseason back at the drawing board at the most important position on the field. The best-case scenario is that Cousins thrives and reworks his deal to replay the role he played with the Raiders this season if the Vikings try yet again to hit on the QBOTF in the draft. Or maybe they can swing a risky trade for Justin Herbert and effectively blow up their cap as several key in-house free agents loom. Does a Daniel Jones trade happen? Do people think Geno Smith or Mac Jones could be the answer?

The bill for the Murray-McCarthy mess will eventually come due. Let's hope KOC still has the currency to pay it without breaking a sweat. I've said before that the best-case scenario and the Vikings often go together like hot fudge and sushi. But we're due for a break after botching the quarterback situation as badly as possible, only to go back to where we started. Right? Right?

Kirk Cousins isn't coming back in another three years to save us again. Well, we are the Vikings, so you never know. That wouldn't even crack the Top 20 for the weirdest things to happen to this franchise.

With Vikings fans and the Minnesota district manager at Kohl's basking in the excitement of the Cousins trade and renewed hopes for a Super Bowl run, there is a game to play against the stupid Green Bay Packers.

As much as it pains me to say it, the Packers come into this one on a roll. They thoroughly destroyed the New England Patriots on the road last week and have won five of their last six.

All eyes will be on Kirk Cousins and the Vikings offense. The good news is that as long as Cousins doesn't miss the team bus, it'll immediately be an improvement over what we've seen recently.

Like last season without Micah Parsons, the Packers' defense is good, if not quite elite. He's close to returning but won't play in this one. They're 11th in both points and total yards allowed, but better (Top 5) in yards per play and defensive DVOA. Pass defense is their strength, as they're in the Top 10 in both pass EPA allowed and passer rating allowed. The Packers have also feasted on interceptions and are 2nd in the NFL. Their run defense looks to be middle of the pack across most traditional and advanced metrics. Hopefully this can be exploited early to get Cousins comfortable and open up the play-action pass game he excelled at under KOC the first time around.

I'm genuinely curious how much of the playbook will be open to Cousins with such a short prep, regardless of familiarity. KOC may have commented on this, but if he did, I missed it. As I noted above, however, it may be too much to expect him to just drop in and play as if he never left. Give it some time. What matters is how this offense looks heading into December.

Quarterback Jordan Love has the offense leading the NFL in passing yards per game. This has translated into strong showings for the dangerous receiving trio of Christian Watson, Matthew Golden, and Tucker Kraft. Those three had a field day against us in Week 1, but that was mostly in the first half, before we woke up and realized the season had started. This version will not be rusty and will surely have resolved the communication and coverage issues that plagued the unit two months ago.

The problem for the Cheeseheads is that their running game is abysmal, ranking 32nd, and they don't have an injured Josh Jacobs to lean on. I'd think this works in the Vikings' favor, as it will force the Packers' offense to play the game on our terms. If the pass vs. run dynamic were flipped, or even more balanced, that would concern me a bit more. That said, Love and the Packers' aerial attack are incredibly dangerous, stellar Vikings pass defense or not. We'd better be ready.

There are a lot of things that worry me about this game for the Vikings: Cousins' comfort level, not helped by an offensive line playing particularly well, likely missing Blake Cashman, who is having an All-Pro season, and the Packers' passing offense clicking on all cylinders.

1. Comfortable or not, Cousins can hit an open receiver.

I'm not letting this high wear off. No way, no how. The running game shows some life now that opposing defenses have to worry about the passing game. This, in turn, opens up some much-needed (and desperately missed) Cousins-to-Jefferson/Addison/Hockenson (if he plays) highlights. Just enough to get the job done on Sunday, with improvements to come.

#2732. Robert Saleh says he and Liam Coen are good after "misunderstanding" last year

When the Titans face the Jaguars on Sunday, Robert Saleh and Liam Coen will coach against each other for the first time since their run-in last year, when they exchanged words on the field over something Saleh, then the defensive coordinator of the 49ers, said something about Coen that Coen didn’t appreciate.

Last year, Saleh said that Coen’s team was good at stealing signals, intending it as a compliment for how good they are at recognizing what the opposing defense was doing. Coen interpreted it as an accusation of cheating and confronted Saleh on the field after the game.

Today Saleh was asked about that, and he said he and Coen have spoken and there are no hard feelings.

“Liam’s great, man,” Saleh said. “We’ve had plenty of interactions. The whole thing is what it was, a little misunderstanding, poor words on my part. But they’re doing a great job. I love what they’re doing offensively. They’ve definitely got an identity. And then [defensive coordinator Anthony Campanile] on the other side. Overall as an organization, they’ve done a really nice job.”

Coen’s confrontational approach toward Saleh after the game last year seemed like an overreaction, but no one can dispute that Coen has done a great job building the Jaguars. Saleh knows he’s going up against a good coach on Sunday, and he won’t provide any bulletin-board material.

#2731. Calm before the Carson storm: Pacheco and Fulghum are set for Saturday

CARSON — There was no trash talk and hardly a word between them. Diego Pacheco and Darius Fulghum met for their final face-off Friday with nothing to say to each other, and nothing to prove until Saturday. Fulghum said afterward it was the first time he really got to look Pacheco in the eyes. "I'm ready for the moment," he said. "I'm going to seize it for sure."

Pacheco (26-0, 19 KOs) is 25 years old, ranked No. 6 by The Ring, and fresh off an 11th-round stoppage of Immanuwel Aleem in July, his second fight with Hall of Fame trainer Buddy McGirt in his corner. He is also comfortable in this building. When the Chris Mannix rattled off his record at the venue, Pacheco jumped in to fix the math. "I'm 4-0," he said. "Four knockouts."

Fulghum (15-1-1, 13 KOs) has been confident all week, saying Pacheco made a mistake picking him, but he has stayed away from the insults. The 30-year-old Golden Boy fighter admitted he had a tough 2025, and he sounds like a man who plans to make the most of the second chance. "I feel like people are sleeping on me and I'm ready to wake them all up," he said. At the presser he added that nobody has seen a style like his, and that he has no doubt he is winning.

He also had a message for Pacheco about opportunity. Fulghum said he is grateful Golden Boy gave him the chance to redeem last year, and that fighters who let world title shots pass may not get them back.

#2729. Kenneth Walker did not practice Friday with groin tightness

There is a potentially significant injury situation brewing with the Chiefs.

Head coach Andy Reid told reporters in his Friday press conference that running back Kenneth Walker sat out Friday’s practice after reporting tightness in his groin.

“We’ll just see how he feels through this afternoon with rehab and through tomorrow,” Reid said. “Somewhere he tweaked it. So, we’ll see. ... He was tight this morning.”

While Reid did not explicitly say a game status, it sounds as if Walker will be questionable.

“We’re kind of feeling it out just to see where we’re at with that thing,” Reid said. “We’re not going to put him at risk or anything like that — that’s not how we roll. So, if he can go, he can go. If he can’t, he can’t.”

Walker leads the league averaging 109.0 rushing yards per game. He’s tallied a total of 872 yards with seven rushing touchdowns in eight games. He’s also caught 26 passes for 172 yards with four TDs.

If Walker is not able to play, rookie Emmett Johnson would be in line to start and carry the load.

“Yeah, if Emmett has to do it, then we all have [confidence],” Reid said. “He’s going to play anyway, whether Ken’s there or not there, he’s gonna play. But everybody has confidence in him, yeah.”

Johnson has 111 rushing yards on 26 carries with a touchdown and 14 receptions for 237 yards with a TD so far this year.

Kansas City’s full injury report with game statuses will be released later in the day.

#2728. Lazio, ammenda di 3.000 euro dopo Napoli: due minuti di ritardo

Il Giudice Sportivo sanziona la società a titolo di responsabilità oggettiva per l’avvio posticipato del primo tempo.

Dopo il pareggio per 1-1 a Napoli, la Lazio ha ricevuto un’ammenda di 3.000 euro. Secondo quanto riportato da Giudice Sportivo, Il provvedimento riguarda un ritardo di circa due minuti all’inizio del primo tempo.

La sanzione è stata inflitta alla società a titolo di responsabilità oggettiva, secondo la decisione del Giudice Sportivo.

Nella motivazione del provvedimento, il ritardo di circa due minuti è indicato come ingiustificato. Riguarda l’avvio della gara, non la ripresa dopo l’intervallo.

#2727. Arizona State’s clash with UCF pushed back after travel delays

Arizona State's clash with UCF pushed back after travel delays appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

A nationwide Federal Aviation Administration ground stop forced officials to make adjustments to the kickoff time for Saturday's game. Arizona State football was among the teams directly affected by the unexpected aviation disruption. The Sun Devils now plan to travel to UCF on Saturday after the FAA grounding, and the kickoff time has also been pushed back.

Ross Dellenger of Yahoo Sports reported that Arizona State was initially scheduled to face UCF at 3:30 p.m. ET on Saturday. However, the travel complications led to a two-hour delay, moving the matchup to a new kickoff time of 5:30 p.m. ET.

"UCF-Arizona State is being pushed back two hours to 5:30 ET Saturday," Dellenger wrote

Watch sports LIVE with fuboTV (free trial)

The Big 12 released a statement Friday evening confirming that it was considering adjustments to Saturday's football schedule because of the nationwide FAA ground stop.

"Due to the national FAA ground stop impacting team travel, the Big 12 Conference is evaluating potential changes to Saturday's football schedule," the statement read. "The Conference will continue to monitor the travel disruptions alongside its member institutions and provide updates for Saturday's games."

Meanwhile, Utah also encountered travel complications ahead of its matchup against Arizona. The Utes were reportedly stranded in Salt Lake City and were not expected to depart until Saturday morning.

After a slow start to the season, the Sun Devils currently hold a 5-4 overall record. Meanwhile, UCF also enters the matchup with a 5-4 record and a 3-3 mark in conference play. Saturday's game therefore gives both programs an opportunity to improve their Big 12 standings.

With kickoff now moved to 5:30 p.m. ET, both teams will have to adjust to the unexpected scheduling changes before taking the field in Orlando.
